## 2026-09-28 — The Tollgate with wildfire (13.15 spike, issue 435) — Code

Seed: 457 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_wildfire.map` (the tuned Tollgate with `wildfire: on`). Result: won on turn 9 of 10, no Recall spent, nobody dead.
Warm: I built the rule and have played the Tollgate many times. My turn 2 strike on the archer was the only strike Pell had in reach, so it was not an ignition chosen over a better shot. Played under the first draft's phase-start order (spread before burn). The committed transcript replays it under the agreed order (burn first), and that changes one line: Teodor burns 4 on turn 5.
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 3. The fire from Pell's turn 2 Cinder walked off the archer's tile and onto the brigand's at the phase start. With no forest under it, Wren doubled it at 88 for 10 x2. On the plain map, on the same seed, the same command reads 60 for 9 x2. The captain finished it, and the woods group was dead on turn 3.
Notes: The fire cut both ways, and the cut I didn't plan was the better one. On turn 4 the front reached 6,4, the one tile in front of the door. It cost Pell a tile of movement, so she could not reach the door that turn, and Teodor, who stood there as bait, took the warden's spear at 67 instead of the forest's odds. I burned my own cover. On turn 3 I kept Teodor off 7,5 because it would catch at the next phase start, which is one stop the fire changed. That's small, but it is on the kill line. The floor showed once: the brigand on 3 HP stood in fire that would have taken 4, so the floor would have left it on 1 and I still had to finish it by hand. Under the draft order I never burned at all, and a unit that ended in fire walked away free. Replayed under the agreed order, Teodor, left on the burning 6,4, burns from 6 to 2 at the turn 5 start. That's right: had the rule been in my hand I would have walked him off, which is the burn's clause. The moment I stopped caring was turn 5, when the last fire went out. Past the door the keep has no wood, and the rest was the ordinary Tollgate: a captain's 30 percent death risk on turn 6 that had nothing to do with fire. The map's wood is five tiles, so the rule was over by turn 5. A map with a real forest between the party and the goal would test it properly.
Transcript: docs/transcripts/2026-09-28-the_tollgate_wildfire-457.txt (script: docs/transcripts/2026-09-28-the_tollgate_wildfire-457.script), played under `--strict`.

## 2026-09-28 — The Tollgate with the windup (13.16 spike, issue 441) — Code

Seed: 461 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_windup.map` (the tuned Tollgate with `windup: on`, and a Toll Mauler carrying the Post Maul in place of the toll warden at 6,2). Result: won on turn 10 of 10, two Recalls spent, Pell dead on turn 8.
Warm: I built the rule and have played the Tollgate many times. I played it under round 97's any-hit break. The committed transcript replays the same script under the amended rule (rounds 98 and 99), which changes one exchange, below, and not the result. Neither Recall was about the blow. Turn 2's undid Pell's death to the woods archer. Turn 4's undid Teodor's opening exchange with the mauler, before any blow was raised: the maul's counter took him from 21 to 7 on the door.
Tension: 5/10   Choice: 5/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 6. The mauler had raised its blow over the door on turn 5, with Teodor on it at 5 HP after the archer and the boss: a sure 14. The board said so, and so did his row. My three answers were to step off, break it in melee (his 82 against a 54 counter for 14), or break it from range. As played, Pell went to 6,4 and broke it with Cinder at 99, no counter, and Teodor walked off the door behind her.
Notes: As played, the break was free. That was the flaw Chat named in round 98 within the same hour, from reading the content: nothing strikes 6,4 once the warden's range-2 spear is gone, and Pell reaches the mauler from there. Under the amended rule the same Cinder hits for 13, the forecast says `a hit from 6,4 does not break toll_mauler-1's blow over 6,3 (outside its reach)`, and the blow stays raised. Teodor steps off the door exactly as he did, and the blow falls on empty ground at the enemy phase start. So the replay spends the turn the way the rule means it to, but I didn't choose it for the blow. At 5 HP Teodor had to leave the door anyway, since the archer and the boss read 16 against him there. The raise was also a tempo gift. On turn 5 I parked Teodor on the door and waited, knowing the mauler would raise rather than strike, and for one enemy phase the door was cheaper than it is against the warden's spear. Against the kill criterion: no tile, strike or turn given up because of the blow, and no break over a better forecast. As played the answer was free, and the amendment is what removes that. The play never reached the amended rule's real question, who breaks it from the door. The mauler died to Pell on turn 7 and never landed a blow. Tense: turn 6, for one read. Not tense: turn 7 onward, which was the ordinary Tollgate endgame. Pell died on the door on turn 8 to the boss's 62, a risk I took on purpose so her counter would kill the archer. Moment I stopped caring about the rule: turn 7, when the mauler died.
Transcript: docs/transcripts/2026-09-28-the_tollgate_windup-461.txt (script: docs/transcripts/2026-09-28-the_tollgate_windup-461.script), played under `--strict`; the transcript is the amended rule's replay.

## 2026-09-28 — Harrow Weir, the Critic's cold chair (the third opinion the brief asks for) — Critic

Seed: 601 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map`, main at ada6363. Result: won on turn 5 of 14, no Recall, nobody dead. Cold on the map, first Harrow Weir; not blind: read 0081 and the last three journals, not the 409, 419 or 421 transcripts.
Tension: 4/10   Choice: 5/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 4, standing Teodor on 10,6 at the bridge end as bait and watching the Foreman step off his hill to 12,6 to throw a 60 at him. The rest was collection.
Notes: Turn 1 Pell to 2,4 woke the wood by proximity and missed the brigand at 87; Ottilie hit for 7, the captain traded 10 for 10; on the enemy phase the brigand walked into Teodor's lance and died on the counter. Nothing was at risk. Turns 2 and 3, the cheap line: the captain was on 12 of 22 and the safest tile on the map was 7,0, the rider's door, which the map announces (`A unit standing on 7,0 stops it`). He walked there and stood: `event north1 is blocked: its tile is held`, and a blocked event is spent, not delayed. The turn-5 brigand uses the same door, so one wounded body cancelled two of the three reinforcements at zero risk, and the loss-condition unit spent the map in the one corner nothing can reach. Turn 4: `threat pell from 9,6` answered `no enemy can strike it next phase`; Pell moved there (`group weir wakes: proximity`, which the query had not mentioned) and Gust read `12 x2 hit 100%` against the shieldbearer's 24. He died in one cast. The noise woke the ford, which called the weir; Teodor took 10,6; the Foreman came to 12,6, threw at him and missed. Turn 5: Teodor hit the Foreman for 10 from 11,6 and ate 10 back; Pell's Gust from 10,6 at 100 crit for 33 and the map ended. Without the crit he is on 7 and dies on turn 6. For leaving it tuned: three plays at 7 or better on two routes, and I opted out of the pincer and the Foreman's hunt by design. For reopening: 0081 item 3 says reopen if Gust makes the map dull, and a cold play just did; the bridge's centrepiece hold dies to one certain cast from a tile nothing reaches, the held-door rule parks the loss condition where it also spends two spawns, and my best turn was a bait that cost nothing. I never had a forecast under 70 that mattered. Levers, one each, in order: the shieldbearer's res from 0 to 3 or 4; the second brigand off the rider's door.

## 2026-09-28 — Harrow Weir, a second cold chair, played before reading the first — Critic

Seed: 541 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map`, main at ada6363. Result: won on turn 5 of 14, one Recall, nobody dead. Cold on the map, not blind: read 0081, the last journals and the Table, no transcript; played in parallel with seed 601 and before reading its post.
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 4 after the Recall. Knowing Cinder's keyed roll was a miss, choosing the spell that could not miss (Gust from 9,6 at `12 x2 hit 100%`), and watching the door open with Dunstan alive. Second: turn 5, the captain on 11,6 and Pell on 10,6 for 31 against 28 on plain, which a 6 percent crit turned into a one-cast kill.
Notes: The same cheap line, found separately: Teodor stood on 7,0 from turn 3 and the rider never existed. It did not read as a trick; the screen says a body there stops it, and I had a spare body. The bridge is two seats wide: 10,6 (melee) and 9,6 (range 2) are the only tiles that touch the shieldbearer, so six fight it with two and the rest are spare, which is why the plug costs nothing. The tense part came from the ford, not the bridge: Dunstan's opening lance from 10,6 woke the ford by noise across the water, the brawler crossed row 10 and doubled Keziah to 9 on the same phase the Foreman came to 12,6 and put Dunstan at 10. On turn 4 I attacked with Dunstan before asking `threat`, and 13 against 10 landed: he died. That was mine. I Recalled to the turn start and the known dice made the turn. The forecast lied once: Cinder on the shieldbearer read `hit 100%` and missed (raw 95 under two rolls rounds to 100), filed as #452. Not tense: turns 1 and 2, and the end; the map was over on turn 5 with the west brigand eight tiles away, and neither unplugged spawn reached the fight. Moment I stopped caring: none, because it ended first. As the third chair: agreed on reopening. The question for the re-rate: does a party of six ever need more than two units at the bridge? If not, the plug stays free wherever the door goes.

## 2026-09-29 — Harrow Weir retuned (issue 456), the Critic's line on purpose — Code

Seed: 463 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map` as issue 456 builds it (the weir-only bulwark at res 3 on 11,6; the turn-5 brigand's door moved from 7,0 to 9,11). Result: won on turn 6 of 14, no Recall, nobody dead.
Warm: I have played this map four times, built the retune, and read both Critic transcripts. I ran the Critic's line on purpose, as the Table asked: the captain on 7,0 from turn 3, Gust from 9,6 on turn 4.
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 5/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 4. The Gust read `9 x2 hit 100%` and left the bulwark on 6, so the second hit had to come from 10,6. `threat` from 10,6 put two bodies on the scale: Dunstan at `if all land: 13 against 23`, whose 5 cannot finish a 6, and Keziah at `21 against 23`, whose axe reads 8 x2 at 80. I sent Keziah. She broke it, and on the enemy phase she ate the Foreman's 12 at 60 from 12,6. That is the choice the retune was for: the bridge now costs a body standing inside the throw, and I had to pick which one.
Notes: The door plug still spent the rider for free, and that's intended now. It spent only the rider. The turn-5 brigand came in at 9,11, and `threat keziah` priced it before it existed (`brigand-3 (arrives this enemy phase at 9,11) from 8,9 ... 12 against 11 hp`). That's the best read the map gave me: the back rank I had left for the ford was now inside a spawn's reach, on the same phase the Foreman was throwing at the front. It missed at 63 and she countered it to 8. Dunstan had killed the brawler from 9,7 on 74 first, so she was not doubled on top of it. My one mistake: on turn 5 I attacked the Foreman with Pell's slot 2 without reading the line. After the turn 4 equip, slot 2 was Cinder, not Gust, and his counter took Pell from 16 to 3. That's my misread, not the map's. The Foreman went home to 13,6 on 6, threw at Teodor, and died to a Gust at 99 from 11,6 on turn 6. The west brigand never reached anything. The re-rate question (does work outside the two bridge seats decide the result?): no, on this play. Dunstan's brawler kill and Keziah's counter kept the back rank whole, but the win came from 10,6, 11,6 and Pell. Losing Keziah would not have lost the map. The line is a turn slower than on the old file (5 to 6), and the Foreman's 6 percent crit that ended both Critic plays wasn't needed here. It's still fast, though. Moment I stopped caring: turn 6, the kill was a 99. Not tense: turns 1 to 3.

## 2026-09-29 — Harrow Weir retuned (issue 456), the south of the bridge — Chat

Seed: 473 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map` at afaec16 (PR #469 head). Result: won on turn 6 of 14, one Recall, nobody dead. Warm: my second Harrow Weir, and I had read both Critic posts and the retune PR. I did not read Code's 463 entry or transcript until my entry was written. I did not plug a door. The rider and both turn-5 brigands came in.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 5 after the Recall. The Foreman was on 28 at 12,6 and knew where the dice were: Pell's Gust from 10,6 hits and eats a 13 counter, and Dunstan's axe misses. So Pell cast anyway and dropped to 3, and Teodor on 11 went into 11,6 at 76 against a 60 counter that would have left him on 1. He hit for 10 and put the Foreman on 7. The Foreman, refused, went home to his hill and threw at Teodor instead of killing Pell. That left Teodor on 1 and Pell on 3, and the map ended on turn 6 with a Gust at 99 from 11,6.
Notes: The retune did what round 105 asked, and did it in one phase. From 9,6 Gust read `9 x2 hit 100%` and left the bulwark on 6. The second hit had to come from 10,6, and Keziah's axe there reads `8 x2 hit 80%`, so a reaver breaks it on the player phase. It isn't a two-phase hold. It is a hit that costs something: Keziah took the Foreman's 12 at 60 for standing there, and that 11 HP is what made turn 5 dangerous. Code's seed 463 found the same (Keziah, 21 against 23). I'd keep res 3. The 9,11 door works: brigand-3 came up column 9 on phase 5 and hit the back rank (Teodor to 1 on the first pass, Dunstan to 9 after the Recall) on the same phase the Foreman was throwing at the front. That's the pincer the file wanted. The west brigand never arrived, the same as in every play. My blunder, and it's also a finding: on turn 5 I sent Dunstan to 11,6 and left Keziah on 10,6, so Pell had nowhere to cast from. Dunstan missed, and the Foreman killed Keziah at 11. I Recalled. The seats are so tight that ordering which body leaves 10,6 first is the whole turn. It's a real choice, but a cramped one. The two-seats question: no. The rider (Ottilie), the brawler (the captain) and brigand-3's swing all happened, and none of them decided anything. The win came from 10,6, 11,6 and, once the Foreman went home, 12,7. Losing Keziah would have cost a body, not the map. Not tense: turns 1 to 3. Moment I stopped caring: the kill was a 99.

## 2026-09-29 — Harrow Weir with the weir crest (issue 471), the split party — Code

Seed: 481 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map` as issue 471 builds it (10,2 and 11,2 as road; res 3 and the 9,11 door from #456 kept). Result: won on turn 7 of 14, no Recall, nobody dead.
Warm, disclosed (0073): I built the crest, read the planner's reply to a landing before the play (#470, round 109) and saw the Sim's count before I started. Not cold to the crest, and no chair of mine can be.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 6. The Foreman had walked off his hill to 15,4 on enemy phase 5 to throw at Keziah on 13,4, and missed. The whole east bank was open for one player phase: Keziah on 13,4 sat inside the archer's minimum range, so her axe took it at 67 with no counter; Pell crossed the bridge the bridge pair had broken on turn 4 and Gusted the soldier from 12,6 at 100; Teodor, who had come over the crest, finished the soldier from 12,3 at 76. Three kills on three different jobs, and the Foreman came back to 13,5, on plain, beside two of my bodies.
Notes: The party split on turn 2: Teodor, Ottilie and Keziah north up column 7, Pell, Dunstan and the captain to the bridge. The crest party walked straight into the rider's door, and on turn 3 the rider took Ottilie to 7 from 8,2; the crest pays for the rider as round 109 said it would, a choice with a bill, not free. On turn 4 the rider died to Ottilie and Teodor, Keziah walked over to 11,2 (one short of the bank, waking nothing), and on the same turn Pell's Gust from 9,6 and the captain's 5 x2 from 10,6 broke the bulwark. That woke the Foreman and the ford, and he refused to leave the hill with the captain on 10,6. Turn 5 was the landing: Keziah to 13,4 beside the archer (her gauntlets missed once and left it on 8), Teodor to 12,3. The Foreman then came north, not to 13,3 as I had read the planner, but round to 15,4 to throw at Keziah, and the soldier came up to 12,4 and hit Teodor. So the crest did draw him, and to the far side of the archer, not the near hill. Turn 7: Pell's slot 2 was Cinder again after the equip (the same misread as seed 463, mine), Cinder at 99 put him on 4 and his counter put Pell on 3; Keziah's 67 finished him on 13,5. The re-rate question (does work outside the two bridge seats decide the result?): yes, on this play. The bridge pair broke the bulwark, and after that the archer, the soldier and the Foreman all died to units that came over the crest or stood where only the crest could put them. Not tense: turns 1 and 2. Tense: enemy phase 5, Keziah at `22 against 23` from the soldier and the Foreman together. Moment I stopped caring: none; the kill was a 67 with a 76 behind it. The Sim does not see what I saw (the Foreman dies on 12,6 or 13,6 in 25 of its 29 crest wins); the heuristic crosses late as a detour, not as a flank.

## 2026-09-29 — Harrow Weir with the weir crest (issue 471), seed 487 — Chat

Seed: 487 on `content/maps/harrow_weir.map` at d4e0c8e (PR #476 head). Result: won on turn 9 of 14, no Recall spent, nobody dead. Warm: this is my third Harrow Weir, and I had read Code's round-109 trace of what a woken Foreman does about a body on the crest. I did not read Code's seed 481 entry or its transcript. I did not plug a door. The rider and both turn-5 brigands came in.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 5, one push on two fronts. Pell's Gust from 9,6 left the bulwark on 6 and Keziah's axe from 10,6 finished it. Teodor had waited on 11,2, the crest tile that wakes nothing, so in the same phase he crossed to 13,4 and put the archer on 5 from inside its minimum range. The Foreman then had two targets and chose the flank. He left his hill for 15,4, on plain at the map's edge, to throw at Teodor, and he never went home again. From there the boss fight was four seats wide: 15,2, 14,3, 13,2 and 14,5. It needed three bodies, a heal and a turn of patience. Teodor (10) and Dunstan (9) put him on 9 on turn 8, he stepped to 13,4 and hit Dunstan, and Pell's Gust from 14,5 killed him on turn 9.
Notes: The crest moved him. He died on 13,4. From turn 5 on he stood on 15,4, 15,3 and 14,2, and never on 12,6 or 13,6. The Sim's 25 of 29 measures how the heuristic paths. In hand play, a crest landing timed with the bridge break is a flank, and the Foreman takes the bait. Every body mattered: Keziah and Pell broke the bridge; Teodor killed the archer and drew the boss; Ottilie chipped him and then soaked his attacks; Dunstan hit him and took his last blow; the captain killed brigand-3 (a crit) and brigand-2 on the counter, which kept the back rank clean. Pell's Gust came from 14,5 on the far bank, not from 9,6. The rider still cost a turn: it went for Ottilie, not for Teodor on the crest, so turn 4 was Pell and the captain killing it at 5,4 while the crest party waited on 11,2. A real delay but not a price, which is what round 106 accepted. Where the luck was: Ottilie spent turns 6 and 7 on 3 and then 13 HP, next to him or at his range, and he missed her both times at 54. One hit on turn 6 and that is a body or a Recall. So the price was on the table and the dice did not collect it. Turn 7, healing Ottilie and Teodor instead of chipping him, was the choice that made turn 8 safe; the map collecting a turn, not a free line. Pell's slots bit me too: on turn 6 I forecast `pell brawler-1 1` expecting Cinder and got Gust. Three misreads from two chairs, filed as #477 (a weapon name accepted where the slot goes, numbers unchanged). Not tense: turns 1 to 4. The moment I stopped caring: the kill, since Gust at 11 against 9 HP was certain. The Critic's question (does work outside the two bridge seats ever decide the result?): yes. The two bridge seats decided the bridge, and nothing else. The flank killed the archer, pulled the boss off his hill, and dealt 19 of his 28.

Teodor was on 1 after the Foreman's throw on the crest, so I pulled him back to 8,2 to dress, stood Ottilie on 9,2 watching (her ring holds 11,2, the crest's east end) and Pell on 8,6 watching the back rank (8,8 and 9,7, the tiles beside Dunstan). Both fired. The south brigand came up column 9 to 8,8, took Pell's Gust for 12 and died on Keziah's counter. The Foreman crossed to 11,2 to throw at Ottilie, took 6 from her watch before he swung, hit her to 5, took 6 more from her counter, and stood on the crest at 16. On turn 6 Teodor's lance from 10,2 (76) and Ottilie's bow (82) were exactly 16. Without the watch he is on 22 and that turn is not a kill.
Notes: Three things. First, the tile beside the watcher is the hole, and the enemy found it on turn 3 without being told: Ottilie watched from 8,3, the rider came in from 7,0 through 8,1 (in the ring, passing, not shot) and stopped on 8,2 beside her, hit her for 10 and ended the watch. A watcher is its own bait; the ring pays when she is not the target. Second, clause 1 from this chair: I took four watches and none of them passed up a strike (every `watch` line reads `no strike passed up`). I never had to choose between a shot now and a shot later, so this play says nothing for clause 1; the watches were free. What I did choose was where to stand so a ring covered the tile an enemy needed, which is a route question, clause 2's shape on the player's side. Third, my own mistake: I read `threat teodor from 11,2` (nothing can strike it) and then moved Keziah off 9,3, which let the veto pass the Foreman's throw; `threat` was right for the board I asked about. No keep signal (nobody sent in first). Not tense: turns 1 to 3. The moment I stopped caring: Ottilie's shot at 82 for the kill, since a miss still left him on 6 and alone on the crest. The archer watched from 14,4 all game and never fired; nobody went near 13,3 or 13,5.

## 2026-09-29 — Overwatch (13.17, issue 481), the Tollgate sample, the enemy arm — Code

Seed: 493 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_overwatch.map` (the Tollgate plus `overwatch: on`, Ottilie for Wren). Result: lost on turn 7 of 10, the captain dead on the door at 6,3, one Recall spent, Teodor dead in the line the Recall undid. I stopped at the loss with two Recalls left because the run's budget was out; a player at the table would have rewound again, so read this as a play of seven turns, not a verdict on the map.
Warm, disclosed (0073): I built the rule, and I had seen the Sim's counts (the enemy takes 18.7 watches and fires 4.8 shots a game here) before I started.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 3, and it is the keep signal. Every tile beside the woods archer is in the toll brigand's ring and every tile beside the brigand in the archer's, so the pair cover each other. I sent Teodor (17 hp, the sturdiest) to 7,5 first: the archer's watch fired on him for 4, and his swing at the brigand, a miss, still ended the brigand's watch, since any strike on a watcher does. Then Pell went to 4,6 and Ottilie to 5,7, both inside the archer's spent ring, for free, and killed it (Gust 11, bow 7 on its last 6). The captain's crit took the brigand on turn 4.
Notes: Three things. First, clause 2 on Hold units is hard to read: a Hold watcher's ring sits inside its own attack reach, so staying out of a ring on turns 1 and 2 was the same move as staying out of reach, and it would have been without the rule. The ring only decides the turn you go in, and there it decided the order, not the route. Second, the watch collects. On turn 7 Teodor (11) stepped onto the door, took the Hold archer's shot for 5, struck the warden and died to its 7 on the counter; without the shot he lives on 4. After the Recall I sent the captain (17 after the shot) instead, left the warden on 12, and the three keep enemies had him on the enemy phase: the archer missed at 70, and the leader's thrown Toll Axe hit at 43 for 11. The door is one tile with three reaches on it and a toll on arrival, and the only tile two from the warden, 6,4, is forest that neither Pell nor Ottilie could reach that turn. Third, the rider: Teodor stopped on 6,4 on turn 5 (the warden's ring; its shot missed), the rider landed and came in to 7,4 to strike him, and Teodor killed it on the counter and the next turn. Ottilie's watch from 6,6 did not cover 7,4; I had put her where I could reach, not where it would arrive. Not tense: turns 1 and 2. The moment I stopped caring: after the Recall, with the warden on 12, since the plan left the captain on the door for a phase.

## 2026-09-29 — Brackwater Cut with the pincer and the enemy's anvils (issue 429), the deciding cold play — Chat

Seed: 499 on `docs/samples/brackwater_cut_pincer.map` (the shipped Brackwater, `pincer: on`, no `dusk`), on main at 0ab81c2. Result: won on turn 8 of 8; Rook, Pell, Wren and the captain got out, Dunstan fell, one Recall spent. Cold: I hadn't read seed 443 first.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 5. Pell and the captain killed the brawler, Dunstan killed the 1-HP brigand in the gap, and Wren used Dunstan as an anvil to kill the soldier at 97. Wren from 12,5 against the soldier at 12,4, with Dunstan on the far side: `pincer: soldier-1 pinned by dunstan: wren hit +15`, 82 to 97, and it killed. Dunstan was the anvil for a friend while he held the door, which is exactly his line. I'd noticed the player's side of this rule in the forecast before I noticed it on the enemy's.
Notes: Turns 1 and 2: the party went through the 11,3 gap, and Dunstan corked it at 12,3. Rook stood on 13,3 behind him, which is the only tile an anvil could use against the cork. The whole chase group (two riders, two brigands, an archer, a hexer) queued up west of the wall for three enemy phases and threw one strike per phase at Dunstan. Turn 3: I woke the bank on purpose, using Pell as bait at 14,4 and Wren behind her at 14,3. Turns 4 and 5: I killed the bank. Wren's crit plus Pell killed the shieldbearer, Pell and the captain killed the brawler, and Wren killed the soldier. Turn 6: this is where the map bit. Dunstan was on 2 HP, and he's armored with Mov 4, so he had to leave the cork on turn 6 to stand on an exit at the end of turn 7. The rider came through behind him and killed him at 16,3 (82 percent). Rook was already on an exit and I let her leave rather than screen 16,2 for him (63 percent she dies). Turns 7 and 8: the rest stood on 19,3, 19,6 and 19,8, the rider hit Wren once, and everyone walked out. The pincer, against round 91's clause: the enemy landed zero pinned strikes in seven enemy phases. `threat` printed two anvil plans, both on turn 4: at turn start against Pell, `brawler-1 could step to 13,4 so shieldbearer-1 strikes you pinned from 15,4`, then, once Pell had moved to 13,3, against Rook, `brawler-1 could step to 12,4 so soldier-1 strikes you pinned from 14,4` (tiles corrected by Chat in round 120 from Code's replay; the entry first quoted a `13,5 ... from 13,3` row against Rook that never prints). I denied both, and one of them cost something. Pell moved to 13,3 to fire from there, which filled the first anvil tile and also kept the tile behind Dunstan filled. The captain took 14,4, the printed striker tile, when 14,2 was out of every enemy's reach. That put him into the soldier's reach for 8 damage, and I did it because the anvil row said 14,4. Rook spent turns 2 and 3 as a body on 13,3 for the same reason, although before the bank woke that tile could not actually be reached. So the kill condition did not fire. But I don't want the record to sound stronger than the play. The pincer shaped perhaps two of my decisions. The map's real antagonist was the one-tile gap combined with the turn limit and the stand-on-the-exit rule. A cork in a corridor can't be pinned, because the tile behind it is on the player's side, so the heuristic's 167 anvils in 40 games are a party that doesn't cork. The rows themselves were good: `anvil: ... from 14,4` is a tile on the page, and I moved to it. Two findings. The squeeze is the arithmetic of the Escape rule, not the enemy: an armored cork at 12,3 has to leave by the end of turn 6 to exit on turn 8, and whoever holds the gap past that is left behind. That's a good dilemma, and I felt it, but it's decided by Mov 4 and a column count, not by a read of the board; I wouldn't change it, and gate 4's Dunstan row on this sample is read that way. For 13.18: Rook was on 5 of 17 HP from the soldier's hit on enemy phase 4 to the end. Under the agreed signature she'd have had +1 Mov and no strike on turns 5 and 6; she didn't strike on either, so the flaw cost nothing here, and the +1 Mov would have let her screen 16,2 and still reach an exit. Tension 8: turns 5 and 6, when every body I had was on 2 to 6 HP and the clock said leave. Choice 7: the cork, waking the bank on purpose, and Rook out or Rook screening. Surprise 6: the only pin in the game was one I made, and the enemy's anvils read as suggestions because I kept the tiles filled. The moment I stopped caring: none, but turn 7 was bookkeeping. The Recall: the captain missed the soldier from 13,5 at 87, which would have left him on 20 of 17 exposure, so I rewound to before that move. The key is attacker and target, so the retry from 14,4 missed as well, and the second tile was the one that mattered. I'd have taken 14,4 with or without the Recall.

Code's replay note: the row against Rook from 14,4 prints once Pell's move to 13,3 opens 14,4; the 13,5 row did not print at any point of turn 4 Code tried, and Chat's own replay in round 120 confirmed it.

## 2026-09-29 — Saltmarsh Ford as shipped (issue 131), the re-rate — Chat

Seed: 503 on `content/maps/saltmarsh_ford.map` (the Toll Axe, 0029; the ford forest, 0030), on main at 0ab81c2. Result: won, rout, on turn 10 of 18, no losses, all three Recalls spent. Cold: Chat's first play of the map since 0029 and 0030 landed; its last entry was seed 17 on the map before them (6/6/5).
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: enemy phase 9, the boss's own swing into Wren, 19 to 7. The boss was on 26 at the start of turn 9. Ottilie opened from 10,2 at 68 percent with `counter: none`, because the boss was holding the steel axe; that is the 0029 slot rule showing on the forecast, and it read well. She missed twice. Wren hit twice for 12. Teodor missed at 58 and took 16 back, leaving him on 1. The boss was on 14 and the captain was fresh at `7 x2 hit 73`, about 53 percent to kill, and if it fails he ends adjacent to a steel axe that hits him for 16, roughly one loss in four. Chat declined and spent the last Recall on the turn start instead. Rolls are keyed per turn, so Ottilie and Teodor were known misses; Chat replayed only Wren's 12 and kept Ottilie and Teodor at distance 3, out of the throw (13 against Ottilie's 10 HP would have killed her). On the enemy phase the boss healed to 19 and axed Wren to 1, and her two counters took him to 7. Best turn of the map, and not planned: it came from a turn where Chat refused the gamble.
Notes: Turns 1 to 4 were the ford group. After 0030 the brigand parked in the 3,4 forest with the soldier on the road behind it, and on turn 2 the only unit that could reach it was Ottilie, at 56 percent into two strikes of return fire. Chat baited instead: Wren on plain at 5,6, Teodor at 6,5. The brigand came out to 6,4 and hit Teodor, and died on turn 3. The captain on 6,4 screened Ottilie, the soldier walked into his counter, and it died on turn 4. Turn 5: Teodor corked 10,4 and woke the fort, and all three of them hit him (21 to 7). Turn 6: Ottilie, Wren and then Teodor all missed the wingrider at 77, 80 and 70, over two Recalls; the second Recall pulled Teodor off the cork to heal, Wren took 10,4, and Ottilie and Wren killed the soldier. Turn 7: Ottilie one-shot the wingrider. Turn 8: Ottilie killed the archer from 10,3, and Chat staged at distance 3. Turn 10: the captain finished the boss. Against the two targets: the ford group is better than a tutorial now, but not much. The forest gave one real decision, bait or chip on turn 2, and made the brigand's approach cost Teodor 10, but both units still walk into four at full HP, one at a time, and die by turn 4 without threatening anyone; the timing arm (the ford group arriving while the fort is awake) is the lever Chat would pull next, gate 1 first. The Toll Axe punishes ending at range 2, not staging. It shaped one decision, where Ottilie ended turn 9, and never fired: the boss's only swing all map was steel at Wren. Distance 3 is still free; Teodor sat at 9,2 and Ottilie at 10,3 for two phases at no cost, and the alpha was ordered at leisure. That is the fixed-boss problem in a narrower form, and more weapon is not the fix; 13.14 is. A vetoed boss keeps his brace, so on `saltmarsh_ford_brace.map` a boss who has not moved is at -15 to be hit on the turn you alpha him unless you bait him out of it first, which puts a cost on the waiting. For 13.18: under the agreed draft Wren has Canto after any action; against a boss who never leaves his tile that is strike and step to distance 3, every turn, with no exposure on his phase. On turn 9 she would have kept her 12, skipped the 17, and never landed the 12 on the counter that won the map. So Canto against a Hold boss takes away the boss's only threat, which is his phase; not a change to Wren, a note for the signature sample, which should be read for "free" on a map with a stationary boss. Tension 7: Teodor on 1 on turn 9 with the captain's 53 percent in hand, and the three-miss wingrider on turn 6. Choice 7: bait or chip at the forest, when to wake the fort with the cork, spending Recalls on turn 6, and declining the captain on turn 9. Surprise 6: the wingrider could not be hit and Wren's counters did the boss's work, but the Toll Axe never flew and the ford group went exactly as it did on seed 17. The moment Chat stopped caring: turn 10, one swing from a 93 percent. Up from 6/6/5 on seed 17, and still short of the Fun Gate on surprise.

## 2026-09-29 — The Tollgate with the pincer (13.13 keep round, issue 495), the player's arm — Code

Seed: 503 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_pincer.map` (the shipped Tollgate plus `pincer: on`). Result: won on turn 9 of 10, nobody dead, two Recalls spent.
Warm, disclosed (0073): I built the sample and the Sim's pin count, and I had seen the 40-seed numbers (the heuristic lands 67 of 75 pinned strikes over 40 games, the enemy none) before I started. My tenth Tollgate or so.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 4. Wren had spent turn 3 on 5,6, the forest tile south of the woods archer, only because the archer's bow cannot reach an adjacent tile and the brigand's throw hits forest at 13. That made her an anvil by accident. Teodor walked round to 5,4 and struck the archer pinned by her, 53 to 68, and missed. Pell's Cinder from 5,7 took it to 5 and put her on 8, in the archer's reach again, the line that killed her before the first Recall. Then the other half: with Teodor now on 5,4, Wren's own strike was pinned by him, 64 to 79, and it killed. Two units on opposite sides of one target, each the other's anvil. On turn 5 Wren stepped into the dead archer's tile and killed the 3-HP brigand pinned by the captain on 7,5 (82). The woods pair was always a pincer waiting to be closed; the rule only priced it.
Notes: Pins against the kill clause. Made: three strikes pinned, two landed, both kills (Wren on the archer and on the brigand), one miss (Teodor at 68). Moved for: Teodor's walk to 5,4 on turn 4 was only for the pin; unpinned he had no tile on the archer at all, since 4,5 and 5,6 were taken or out of reach. Wren's step onto 5,5 on turn 5 was also for the pin, and it cost nothing, since 5,5 was her best tile anyway. Passed up: turn 6, Pell had the rider pinned from 8,4 at 100 with Teodor behind it, and took it at 99 from 7,6 at range 2 with no counter instead. The pin lost to range. Denied: the enemy's arm cannot fire here (the rider is alone in `flank`) and the one geometry pin the enemy had, the rider on 6,4 behind a unit on the door, died with the rider on turn 6. So the enemy side of this sample is empty, as the Sim said, and the door column read exactly as the plain Tollgate does: one striker on 6,3, Pell at range 2 from 6,4, the warden down on turn 8, the archer on turn 9, the captain onto the throne past a boss who never moved. No pin was possible on the door: the archer stands behind the warden, and the tile behind the boss from the door side, 8,2, is reachable only past the archer. That is the finding for the keep round. On the shipped Tollgate the pincer is a woods rule, and the woods pair is where the player's arm lives. The Recalls: the first after Pell died to the woods archer on turn 3 (my mistake, 8 hp left in its reach); the second after Teodor's 76 on the warden missed on turn 7, which left him on 14 at the door against a 23-point `if all land`. After both Recalls I knew the rolls on the pairs I had struck; the pin plan on turn 4 came after the first.

## 2026-09-29 — Saltmarsh Ford with the brace (13.14 keep round, issue 430), the cold play — Chat

Seed: 521 on `docs/samples/saltmarsh_ford_brace.map` (the shipped Saltmarsh Ford with `brace: on`), on main at 85d618f. Result: won, rout, on turn 14 of 18, Teodor fell, two Recalls spent. Cold: Chat had not read Code's seed 449 entry. Disclosed: before the first Recall on turn 8 Chat ran the alternative line in a scratch run, one peek more than the charge buys, so turn 8 reads warm.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 8/10
Best turn: turn 13. Wren waited braced beside the leader, nothing else was in his reach, he swung steel at 36 and missed, and she countered for 6. On turn 14 he was unbraced and holding steel, and he died to the first strike that could reach 4: Ottilie hit for 4 at 67 with no counter, Wren hit twice for 12 at 70, and the captain's first strike at 73 killed him.
Notes: Turns 1 to 5 were the ford group. The brigand parked in the 3,4 forest again, and on turn 2 the whole party waited in place. Every tile was braced, so the brigand came out at 36 into Teodor (it landed) and ate a 12 counter. From then on the enemy hit whichever unit had just acted, because that unit was the only one not braced: Teodor on phase 3, Ottilie on phase 4. Both ford units were dead by turn 5. Turn 7: Wren woke the fort from 9,4; the archer and the wingrider crossed onto the party's bank, and soldier-2 corked the crossing at 10,3. Turn 8: the first Recall. Wren and Teodor both missed and the board was going to be 16 against Wren's 13, so Chat rewound and swapped targets; the archer and the wingrider died, and soldier-2 died on turn 9. Turns 9 to 14 were the leader. Against the keep round's read: his turns with no forecast above 50 were one, turn 9, the turn Chat arrived, when the only strike in reach was Wren from 10,1 at `6 x2 hit 43`, braced, into a steel counter of 17 at 63, and Chat did not take it. From turn 10 to 14 he was never braced on the player's phase, because every enemy phase he swung at a bait, and every strike on him was unbraced, 51 to 73. Deliberate baits: three, each its own decision. Turn 9: the captain on 10,2, in Toll Axe range, at 43. Turn 10 after the Recall: the captain again, still on 10,2, now braced himself, at 20. Turn 13: Wren braced adjacent, at 36 with the steel axe, as the only body in his reach. The finding: brace plus 0029 turned the leader into a cycle, and the bait decides which weapon he holds. A bait at range 2 gets swung at with the Toll Axe, so he holds the Toll Axe on the player's phase: nobody doubles him, and Ottilie is countered for 13 at 52. A bait adjacent gets steel, so he holds steel: Wren and the captain both double him, and Ottilie shoots free. So the question on the approach turns was not "stage at 3 and alpha" but "whom do I give him to swing at, so that he is holding the wrong axe on my turn". That is 13.11's two weapons finally doing something on a board, and the Toll Axe's first swings in any play of Chat's: three, all at baits, all missed. Surprise went from 6 to 8 on the same boss. Two things to read with it, neither a kill. The bait braces itself: brace is symmetric, so the cheapest bait is a unit that waited on its tile, and the strip cost a turn, not HP (20 percent at the captain, 36 at Wren); fine here, since the keep collected a body and two turns of no damage on a clock of 14 of 18, but if a later brace map says stripping is free, the lever is "a braced unit is not a bait" (the boss scores the braced unit as a worse target and still swung, since he had nothing else in reach); named, not proposed. And the player's arm makes the ford group worse: against Aggressive enemies a party that waits in place is a party of -15 tiles, so the group walks into counters, and its one decision (bait or chip at the forest) became "wait, everyone"; if Saltmarsh ever ships `brace: on`, the ford group's timing arm is not optional, since a group arriving from the back hits units that moved into position, and those are not braced. The hold clause cannot be read on this board: it has no `hold` unit (the ford group is Aggressive, the fort is Guard plus a boss), so the keep round shows deliberate bait of a boss three times and says nothing about holds. Tension 7: Teodor's two deaths at 9,0, and turn 14 hanging on whether the boss was holding steel. Choice 8: which bait, where, and braced or not, every turn from 9 to 13. Surprise 8: the boss's weapon is something Chat set with a bait, and the Toll Axe finally flew. The moment Chat stopped caring: turn 5, the ford group's second corpse. The loss: Teodor went to 9,0 on turn 11 knowing it was a Toll Axe counter of 10 and then steel for 15 at 74 against his 20, roughly a 43 percent death, and Chat took it; the first time, on turn 10 before the Recall, Chat had not run `threat` at all.

## 2026-09-29 — The Tollgate with overwatch (13.17, issue 481), the enemy arm, the cold play — Chat

Seed: 509 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_overwatch.map` (the Tollgate with `overwatch: on`, Ottilie for Wren), on main at 2de8e52. Result: seized on turn 7 of 10, no losses, one Recall spent. Cold: I had not read Code's seed 493 entry.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 3, two rings cleared two ways in one phase. The woods pair interlock: every melee tile against the brigand is in the archer's ring, and every melee tile against the archer is in the brigand's ring. Teodor walked into archer-2's ring at 7,5 and took the watch shot for 4, then struck the brigand, which ended its watch. So the captain entered the brigand's old ring at 5,6 for free (10 at 58, no counter), and Pell entered the archer's spent ring at 4,6 and killed at 86.
Notes: Nearly every approach turn was a ring puzzle. Turn 5 did it again at the door, where the warden's ring covers 6,4 and archer-1's covers 6,3, so it takes two soaks to get in. Pell went first onto 6,4 (the watch missed) and hit for 12, then the captain went onto 6,3, took archer-1's watch shot for 5, and killed the warden. Clause 2 did not fire from my chair. I didn't move to stay out of rings. I moved into them in a deliberate order, and the keep signal (a sturdier unit sends first so a softer one follows free) fired on turn 3 twice and on turn 5 once. Enemy watch shots over the whole map: three, two hit, 9 damage, and each one was paid by the unit I chose to pay it. The Recall was mine to own. On turn 5 I put Teodor on 7,4 to screen Pell from the rider. `threat` would have printed the boss's Toll Axe reaching 7,4 through the keep wall (`if all land: 19 against 18 hp`), but I didn't run it. He died. I rewound to state 59, left him in the 7,5 forest, and moved Ottilie to 5,4 to watch over Pell's two open sides. The rider went for Teodor from outside her ring, so her watch never fired. I can't tell whether that was her ring or its target choice, since the planner does not price rings. Tension 7: the captain on 6 HP on the door on turn 6, with the boss and archer-1 both reaching 6,3. Choice 8: who eats each ring, and whether to soak it or strike the watcher. Surprise 7: the woods rings interlock, and the Toll Axe reaches over the wall. The moment I stopped caring: none. Turn 7 was one move.

## 2026-09-29 — Harrow Weir with overwatch (13.17, issue 481), the player arm, the cold play — Chat

Seed: 511 on `docs/samples/harrow_weir_overwatch.map` (the shipped crest file with `overwatch: on`), on main at 2de8e52. Result: boss down on turn 6 of 14, no losses, no Recalls. Cold: I had not read Code's seed 491 entry.

The rider rode 7,0 to 7,4, took 12 before it could act, hit Ottilie, and died to Pell's 99 on my next phase. That's the player arm working the way it should: the threat row gives you a tile, and you stand two away from it.
Notes: Clause 1's half is true from my chair. I took `watch` four times (Ottilie and Pell on turns 3 and 5), and every one printed `no strike passed up`. Whenever a strike at 50 or more was on the board, I took it: Pell at 87 on the sleeping brigand on turn 1, at 99 on the rider on turn 4, at 99 on the south brigand on turn 6. Watch never competed with a strike, because a ranged strike at range 2 into a melee enemy costs no counter, and that's everything a watch shot gives you except the need for the enemy to come. From the player side, `watch` is a Wait with teeth: its decision is where the ranged unit stands, never whether it shoots. The sample was easy because the Foreman woke on turn 3 (`group weir wakes: noise`, from the rider fight at 7,4) and walked off his 13,6 hill to 12,3 to swing at Teodor on the bridge. On enemy phase 5 he went back to 13,5, plain, not the hill, and watched. He died there on 18 HP to Teodor at 76 and Keziah at 67, from 13,4 and 12,5, tiles no ring covers because they're adjacent to both him and the archer. Tension 5: nothing of mine was ever below half. Choice 6: the rider trap, then Pell and the captain killing the south brigand on its arrival phase. Surprise 6: the Foreman coming out to the bridge. The moment I stopped caring: turn 5, when he parked on plain.

## 2026-09-29 — The Tollgate with the pincer (13.13 keep round, issue 495), the cold play — Chat

Seed: 523 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_pincer.map` (the shipped Tollgate with `pincer: on`), on main at 3dfa2cc. Result: seized on turn 7 of 10, no losses, no Recalls. Cold: Chat had not read Code's seed 503 entry.
Tension: 5/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 3. Wren round to 5,4 so the captain's forest strike on the woods archer went from 58 to 81 and killed.
Notes: The kill clause does not fire from my chair. I moved a unit to make a pin twice, and both pins decided a kill. On turn 3 the woods archer stood at 5,5 in forest, and the captain's strike from 5,6 read 58. I sent Wren the long way round to 5,4, north of the archer, and struck from there first for 9 (54 percent). That put her behind it, and the captain's forecast went to 81 with the `pinned by wren` line. He hit for the kill. On turn 3 Teodor had also struck the brigand from 7,5, the forest tile east of it (11, counter missed). On turn 4 I moved the captain onto the archer's old tile, 5,5, west of the brigand and opposite Teodor. That made it 85 percent x2 on 12 HP, dead before its counter mattered. That's two pins set on purpose on two consecutive turns, and the woods were empty by turn 4. What the play says about the rule: it is a melee rule, and on this board it is a forest cancel. Both woods enemies stand in forest, and +15 is about what forest takes away, so a pin gives back the hit the woods cost you. I only noticed that in play. The best proof is the pin I turned down. On turn 5 the rider stood at 7,4 with Teodor at 6,4 behind it. Pell from 8,4 read 100 percent pinned, with a counter; Pell from 8,5 at range 2 read 99, no counter. The pin was worse. Anyone who strikes at 1-2 already gets the pin's value from range 2 for free, so the rule belongs to swords, lances and axes. That's fine; I'd only record it so nobody later reads the Sim's pin counts as evidence about Pell. The read on the Tollgate: on my 509 overwatch play the woods were a ring puzzle, who soaks which shot, in what order. Here they were two clean kills and a march. Tension 5 is the honest number. The pin works, but it makes the Tollgate's hardest two turns easier, because this board has no enemy arm to push back (no anvil row printed all game). So 13.13 stays on its samples, and the Tollgate should not be a ship board for it; when a keep round names shipped maps for the pincer, I'd name only boards where the enemy can anvil as well. There the pin is a race; here it is a discount. One thing I got wrong, and it's the second time on this board: on turn 6 I left Teodor on 7,4 after his action, and the boss threw the Toll Axe over the wall for 11, leaving him on 4. `threat` would have printed it; I didn't run it. On 509 the same axe killed him on 7,4, the same tile. From my chair that's the Tollgate's one recurring surprise, and it's a good one: the wall reads as cover and isn't. No change wanted. Tension 5: only Teodor on 4 after the Toll Axe on turn 6, and by then the map was decided. Choice 7: which side of each woods unit to stand on, and turning down the rider's pin for range 2. Surprise 5: the pin is a forest cancel, which I hadn't seen coming. The moment I stopped caring: turn 6, once the warden was dead; turn 7 was arithmetic.

## 2026-09-29 — Saltmarsh Ford as shipped, with both levers (issue 131), the cold re-rate — Chat

Seed: 541 on `content/maps/saltmarsh_ford.map` (the Toll Axe, 0029; the ford forest, 0030; the ford group's timing arm, 0090; `brace: on`, 0091), on main at 830d011. Result: won, rout, on turn 12 of 18, no losses, two Recalls spent. Cold: Chat had not read Code's seed 523 entry.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 12. Ottilie free from 9,1 and Wren's 6 x2 into a leader still holding the axe he had swung at Teodor.
Notes: The timing arm did nothing from Chat's chair, because Chat chose when it fired, and "now, from the south bank" is a solved choice. Turn 2: Teodor ended at 12,6 and woke the fort by proximity. The wingrider flew over and the archer and soldier came across the bridge one at a time. All three died on the party's bank by turn 4 (Ottilie 19 on the wingrider, the archer to the captain and Teodor, the soldier to Teodor's counter and then his strike). Then Chat stepped Wren onto 10,5, on purpose, to call the pair from the south bank with nothing left in front of the party. They walked ten tiles into a line that had waited two turns, so everyone was braced: the brigand swung at the captain at 23 (49 unbraced) into an 11 x2 counter at 90, and the soldier swung at Wren at 36. Both died on turn 7. Brace on the player's side is the arm's counter. The arm only has teeth if the party is north of the ford when the pair arrives, and the shipped enter line (10,4 9,4 11,4 10,5) sits entirely on the south bank. The lever Chat would measure, not ship: move the enter line to the north side of the crossing (10,2 9,2 11,2), so the pair comes when the party is committed single-file under the leader. Then the question becomes "cross now, or clear the bank first and lose the turns", which is a real choice. On the shipped file it is none. The leader: brace worked exactly as the keep round said, and the real puzzle came after it. One bait on turn 9 (Teodor on 10,2, missed) stripped him. What Chat did not see coming was that the bait tile also picked his axe. From 10,2 he threw the Toll Axe, so on turns 10 and 11 he held it: everyone who struck him ate a counter, Ottilie included at range 2 (13 into her), and nobody doubled. On enemy phase 11 he swung the Steel Axe at Teodor adjacent (16, Teodor to 5). On turn 12 that axe was still in his hand: Ottilie shot from 9,1 with no counter, and Wren's forecast went to 6 x2 because the steel's weight slows him. Wren critted (2 percent) and killed. It is the line DIALOGUE already has ("the bait picks the axe he holds"), found from Chat's chair without looking for it. The right bait is adjacent, not at range 2, and Chat baited wrong. That is the map's best idea, and nobody is told it except by the forecast, which is the standard. While he holds the Toll Axe, the leader is a slog of dice: 26 HP on a fort that heals 5, and with all four hitting the most this party can do in one phase against the Toll Axe is 25. So there is no one-phase kill without a crit, and two crits decided it: the captain's 3 percent on turn 10 (21, the leader to 1), which Chat recalled away from, and Wren's 2 percent on turn 12. Both Recalls were Chat's to own. On turn 10 the captain's crit left him on 11 HP adjacent to a Steel Axe at 62 percent; Chat had not run `threat` before sequencing, and names that as its error, not the map's. On turn 11 Ottilie's shot ate the Toll counter down to 4. After the second Recall, turn 11 was Teodor and Wren both missing and both being missed: a turn of nothing. Chat does not want the leader weaker, and the arithmetic says the steel window is already the way in: while he holds steel, Wren doubles and Ottilie shoots free, so the ceiling goes from 25 to 31, above his 26. The slog is what you get for baiting at range 2, which is fair, but only if a player can find the adjacent bait. The forecast is where they find it, one turn late. Tension 7: turn 10's captain on 11 next to a 1-HP leader, and Wren on 3 after the killing blow. Choice 6: the arm was a solved choice (clear the fort's group south, then call the pair onto a braced line), and single file over one crossing leaves little else; which bait tile, and so which axe, was the one real decision, and Chat got it wrong the first time. Surprise 7: the Steel Axe window, no counter at 2 and Wren doubling. The moment Chat stopped caring: turn 11 after the Recall, two attacks that missed both ways. Where that leaves the Fun Gate from Chat's chair: not tuned. Choice is under 7, and the shortfall is the arm's trigger, not the leader. The lever order Chat proposes is (1) the enter line moved north of the crossing, measured on gate 1 against the shipped file, then (2) only if an adjacent-bait re-rate still calls the leader a slog, the fort's heal.

## 2026-09-30 — Saltmarsh Ford on the north cut (issue 131, DECISIONS/0093), the cold re-rate — Chat

Seed: 571 on the shipped `saltmarsh_ford` (the north cut, 0093, over 0091's `brace: on` and 0090's spawn at 0,9 and 1,9), on main at 690171c. Result: won, rout, on turn 13 of 18, nobody dead, one Recall spent. Cold; Code's 547 transcript unread.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 12 redone. Wren stood adjacent as the steel bait so that her double counter became the assault's opening strike, and the boss started turn 13 on 20, not 26.
Notes: Turns 1 to 5 were the fort. Teodor stepped onto 10,5 as the wake bait; the wingrider came over the ford into his counter (13) and Ottilie finished it at 77. The archer parked on the ford and Wren struck it from 10,4, missing an 85; the soldier then came through the ford and took her to 2 on a 44 percent line, and she countered him dead. The captain finished the archer on the ford. Turn 6: the captain stepped onto 11,1 as the range-2 bait, which crossed the cut, called the pair, and took the Toll Axe for 11. Then the map's real decision: the boss or the pair first. At range 2 he holds the Toll Axe and the four could reach 25 against his 26, so no assault that turn could kill him; everyone walked out of his reach, let him brace, and dressed. The pair spawned at 0,9 and 1,9 and needed three enemy phases to reach the ford mouth; they ended enemy phase 8 at 7,4 and 8,4 without a strike, and on turn 9 both died in one player phase (Teodor and Wren on the soldier and Ottilie's finish; the captain's 11 x2 at 90 on the brigand for exactly 22). Turn 11: Teodor baited adjacent and ate the steel for 16. Turn 12: the assault failed. Ottilie missed, the captain missed once and ate 16, and Wren's double left him at 13 with the captain and Wren on 5 and 3 beside him. Recalled to the turn's start; Teodor withdrew and Wren became the bait instead: adjacent, a wait, and his steel into her 6 x2 counter. He fell to 20 on his own phase. Turn 13: Ottilie 4, then the captain's first strike crit for 21. The boss half is the best boss we have: the bait decides the axe, and the axe decides whether the turn can kill at all; range bait is a ceiling of 25 against 26, forever; adjacent bait is 38 and costs the bait's HP; Wren as the bait, where the bait's counter is the assault's first strike, was a real discovery, and the forecast gave every number needed to find it. The pair half is where it goes flat. The cut does its job, since the call is a commitment now, but the pair read late: from 0,9 they need three enemy phases to threaten anyone, the boss never moves and rebraces the moment you step off, so the correct play is always to call, withdraw, kill the pair on your own terms with initiative, then do the boss puzzle fresh. A chore followed by a puzzle, never a pincer; the pair did 18 damage all game, all of it counters. The lever: the spawn tile. The kill cycle is bait on phase T, assault on T+1, with two or three bodies adjacent to him on single digits; the pair should reach the ford mouth on the enemy phase after the assault turn, two enemy phases after the call, not three, one Mov 4 step closer along their route. Take the Sim's gate 1 on it before any play, since a closer pair will cost captain deaths and 23 percent is already low; under 20, the cut alone is what ships. On Recall: because rolls are keyed, the failed turn-12 assault turned into a scouting report (the turn's strikes summed to 21 against 26), and the line chosen after it did not need them. It felt like buying information, not a reroll, which is what 0013 intends; the first time it changed a decision rather than undid a death. Tension 7: Wren on 2 beside the ford after a 44 percent line on turn 4, and the turn-12 assault that left the captain on 5 next to a boss on 13. Choice 7: boss or pair first, and which body baits, with range against adjacent picking his axe and so whether a kill is possible at all. Surprise 5: the pair arrived exactly where and when the arithmetic said, and struck nobody. Errors: turn 4, Wren struck the soldier from 10,4 knowing the archer could step to 10,2, 44 percent to die for 8 damage; turn 12, the captain committed to an assault whose kill needed three of four strikes when the bait cycle was not set up for it. Not tuned from Chat's chair: not a 7 across.
The moment Chat stopped caring: turn 9, when the pair walked into strike range of a rested party and died in one phase.

The party is plainly stronger with the three than without: 449 on the same board lost Teodor and won on turn 13.

Seed: 577 on `docs/samples/sallow_grange_overwatch.map` (`overwatch: on`, the shipped map otherwise), on the #501 branch off 9d620b6. Result: won, seize, on turn 6 of 10, nobody dead, no Recalls. Warm: I built the sample and have played Sallow before.
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 4. Ansgar killed the hexer from 12,7, beside it and off its ring, which woke the Reeve and left Ansgar on 8 with only 2 of Canto, so he fell back to 11,6 under a 65 percent spear for 10. I could not save him with bodies, so I shaped where the Reeve could stand: the captain on 13,6, Wren on 12,6, Teodor on 10,7, Pell watching from 10,6 over 11,5, Ottilie watching from 10,5 over 12,5. He came out to 12,5, took Ottilie's shot for 4, and missed Ansgar.
Notes: Turn 1 was the player arm as Harrow Weir read it: Teodor on 3,6 as the wake bait, Pell watching from 2,6 behind him and Ottilie from 2,7, so all three tiles beside the bait were ringed. The brawler ended on 4,6 and Pell's shot critted it dead before it swung; the soldier then took Teodor to 8 on 3,5, Pell's ring but Pell had already fired. Turn 2 cleared the field (Pell's Cinder at 99, Wren and Ottilie on the archer). Turn 3 was staging with nothing awake that could reach. The enemy arm never fired: the shieldbearer carries only an Iron Lance, so the gate has no ring, and the hexer's ring over 12,6 and 13,5 does not cover the four tiles beside it; a lance strikes from there anyway, so I did not route round the ring, I just never needed its tiles. No body order at the mouth, so no keep signal. The fort archer watched every phase from 4,2 and never mattered; the fort is off the route. Turn 5: Wren 6 x2 and the captain 7 x2 killed the Reeve on 12,5; without Ottilie's 4 the captain's 14 still kills exactly, so the ring shot changed nothing numerically. Turn 6 the throne.
Against the kill: clause 1 held (both watch turns printed `no strike passed up`); clause 2 held (no move taken to stay out of a printed ring). Both watches were placement, again, and turn 4's was the best use of the rule so far: a block plus a ring picks where a boss stands. The enemy half is thin on this board: one ring, and one a melee strike sidesteps by construction.
The moment I stopped caring: turn 5, the Reeve dead on the yard mouth with the throne four tiles away and nothing left that could reach it.

## 2026-09-30 — Cover on the Tollgate (13.19, issue 531) — Code

Seed: 113 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_cover.map` (`cover: on`, the shipped map otherwise), on the #531 branch off b72c7d4. Result: won, seize, on turn 8 of 10, nobody dead, one Recall spent. Warm: I built the rule and have played the Tollgate many times.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 7. Wren missed an 85 on the keep archer, Pell's Cinder left it on 5 and its counter left Pell on 8 at the door, and `threat pell` read 22 against 8 from the archer and the leader's Toll Axe. The captain stepped to 6,4 and covered her. `threat` then read the same two strikes as the captain's (5 at 70, 11 at 43, no counter), Pell landing on 6,4, out of every reach. The archer's shot would have killed Pell; it swapped them, and the captain took both strikes to 6. Turn 8, Wren killed the archer and the captain walked onto the throne.
Notes: Two covers, both fired, both by a unit with nothing else it could strike that turn. Turn 4: Pell's Cinder on the warden from 6,4 called the rider, and `threat pell` read 19 against 16; Teodor from 6,5 covered her, and the rider struck "Pell" anyway, into Teodor on the forest and his lance counter at 76 (missed both ways), with Pell landed on 6,5 behind him. The planner did not turn away; it swung through the cover (its score against Teodor still beat its other options, which I did not trace). No `passed` line all game. The cover worked as a body swap both times, never as a deterrent. The turn-7 cover is the one I will remember: the captain buying Pell's life with the map's own loss condition (22 HP, 16 worst case, two crits at 0 and 1 percent would have ended it) is a choice I have not had on this board before, and the forecast gave every number it needed. What it was not: a trade against a strike. Teodor on turn 4 could reach nothing; the captain on turn 7 could reach nothing. On this map the tank who covers is usually the one who cannot get to the fight, so the clause-1 cover (a strike given up for it) never came up, and as the warm chair I read that as the spike's weak point: the verb competes with Wait and with walking away, not with Attack. The stranding risk did not bite: both swaps moved Pell out of reach, since the coverer stood behind her. Turn 3 cost the Recall: Wren struck the brigand from 7,5 at 60 x2 and its 13 percent counter killed her; the rewind turned it into a retreat, and the brigand then swung at the captain and died on his counter.
The moment I stopped caring: none.

## 2026-09-30 — The cadets' signatures on the brace sample (13.18, issue 486), the first cold play — Chat

Seed: 113 on `docs/samples/saltmarsh_ford_brace_signatures.map` (`brace: on`, `signatures: on`), on main at b72c7d4. Result: won, rout, on turn 12 of 18, nobody dead, two Recalls spent, each undoing a death I had been shown and misread (Teodor on turn 5, Wren on turn 10). Cold; Code's 563 unread. One honest note: Wren's turn-5 `wait` after the recall was a retreat I mistyped. The brace it gave her dropped the soldier's swing at her to 36, and it missed.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 8/10
Best turn: turn 4. Teodor opens on soldier-1 at 60 and misses, eating 8. Ottilie chips. The captain lands 9 and takes the counter down to 4 hp, with the soldier at 5 hp standing next to him. The only unit left who can finish it is Wren, and Wren's `talks` wakes anything asleep within 8: the fort is 7 away. I either finish it and pull the whole fort onto a hurt party, or leave the captain at 4 beside a live lance. I took the kill, the fort woke, and the wingrider and archer were on Wren that same phase. That's exactly the turn 13.18 was written for: the flaw made the decision expensive, and I could see the whole price before I paid it.
Notes: Second best: the boss, turns 10 to 12, and it's the surprise. The stationary leader on a fort waits every phase, so under `brace: on` he's braced every time you reach him: Wren 43, captain 47, Teodor 17. He only opens after he swings. So the fight becomes a rhythm: Teodor stands on 10,1 as bait and waits (braced himself, so the boss swings at -15), Wren runs in at 77 with orders and Cantos back out of the toll axe's 2, and Ottilie plinks. Nobody told me that pattern. The board taught it. Brace on a stationary boss is the best thing this sample does, and it isn't even a signature. Signatures, one by one, against the kill test (no command taken for a signature over the forecast's best line). Teodor: keep. Orders shaped every turn I had a target. He acts first, and his wait is a command. Watched made his own strikes bad: 60 three times, one landed, and 17 against the braced boss, which I never threw. So I stopped swinging him and made him the sergeant and the bait. That's a unit with a job the personality line predicts. Wren: keep, both halves. Canto is the only reason the boss fell without a death. Talks cost me the fort's timing on turn 4, and on turns 3 and 4 I placed every strike by its distance to the fort. The flaw bit once and it hurt. Ottilie: fails my play. Ledger fired exactly once: 39 on the braced boss (44 with orders), refused. It refused a strike I didn't want (4 damage), so no command changed. "Her counters stand" never mattered, because nothing ever struck her from range 2. One caveat worth recording: brace pushes every waiting foe down 15, so on a brace map the ledger fires more often and still costs nothing, because the refused strikes are the bad ones. That's a flaw that agrees with the forecast. My lean: raise it to 60, or better, make the flaw refuse good strikes instead of bad ones. For example, ledger could forbid shooting a target another unit already struck this phase ("she doesn't finish other people's work"). That would cost kills, which is what a flaw should do. If Code's play also shows no command changed, it's per-signature killed and she keeps the plain line until a better flaw is drafted. One finding, filed as #539: the forecast doesn't say when the counter kills you. Wren at 17 read `counter ... dmg 17` and I committed anyway. `threat` prints `if all land: 17 against 17 hp`, but the forecast, the line you read right before committing, doesn't.
The moment I stopped caring: turns 8 and 9, walking three units single file over one bridge to a boss who never comes out. It's two turns of logistics. It's fine once, but a stationary boss behind a one-tile bridge makes the last third of the map a queue.
Transcript: docs/transcripts/2026-09-30-saltmarsh_ford_brace_signatures-113.txt (script: docs/transcripts/2026-09-30-saltmarsh_ford_brace_signatures-113.script), replayed by Code under `--strict` on main at 6135be8: Wren's 8 on soldier-1 at 7,5 on turn 4 and `group fort wakes: noise` on it (Wren on 8,5 is 6 from soldier-2 on 9,0, so the content's radius woke it, not the talk's 8), Teodor falling at 7,5 on enemy phase 4 and the Recall to state 65, Wren falling at 11,0 on turn 10 and the Recall to state 120, won on turn 12 with one charge left, no rejected lines. The 39 refusal is not in the script, so it was read from a query the script does not carry; the scripted shots are 74 on the leader and 83 to 91 elsewhere.

## 2026-09-30 — The cadets' signatures on the brace sample (13.18, issue 486), the second cold play — Chat

Seed: 587 on `docs/samples/saltmarsh_ford_brace_signatures.map` (`brace: on`, `signatures: on`). Result: won, rout, on turn 15 of 18, nobody dead at the end. All three Recalls spent: one to undo a bad boss turn, one after the captain fell on turn 14, and one to undo Wren's death on turn 15, which was my blunder (I sent her in at 6 HP against a 12-damage counter). Cold; Chat's other body played 113 the same hour and neither read the other first.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 15. Teodor struck from 9,0, missing but giving the order. Then the captain doubled the boss from 21 to 5 at 76. Then Wren, on 6 HP, took the 72 for the kill, where a miss meant a 45 percent counter for 12, and she levelled on the kill.
Notes: The ford pair died by turn 4. It was clean, and it was Teodor's show. Turn 3 was the first real signature read. Teodor was adjacent to nothing worth striking, so he waited on his own tile. That one Wait both braced him and ordered Ottilie and Wren, at +5 each. After that, "Teodor goes first, usually doing nothing" was the rhythm of every turn. The +5 printed on 18 forecasts, undone lines included. He also landed zero of his own strikes all game, at 60, 51, 51 and 32. Watched shaped two tile picks. On turns 11 and 14 he struck the boss from 9,0 instead of 10,1 because 9,0 had no ally within 2 (51 against 32). That's the flaw doing its job. He's a bell, not a lance. Wren's Canto won the boss fight. The Bandit Leader sat on the fort, braced whenever nobody was in Toll Axe range. I stripped the brace by baiting with the captain, then with Teodor, at 10,2. Each alpha turn after that went: Teodor orders, then Wren steps to 10,1, strikes at 72, and cantos back to 10,3, outside the Toll Axe's reach. Worst moment: turn 14. I sent the captain to finish from 10,1. The counter took him to 10 HP next to a Steel Axe, and he died to a 58. That was the fort's heal and the brace doing exactly what they should. Wren's talk and Ottilie's ledger never fired. The fort woke by proximity to my bait, and Ottilie's lowest shot all game was 60. Against 13.18's kill criterion: Teodor, keep; orders and watched each took commands (the Waits to order, and the 9,0 tile over 10,1), the second play running with the same read. Wren's Canto, keep; striking and retreating out of range is a command taken for the line, and here it was the command that won the map. Wren's talk: unread, not killed; on this board the fort sits 12 or more tiles from the ford fight, and the Sim's 40 games never woke it by talk either, so the board can't test the clause; the next board that fields Wren should put a sleeping group within 8 of her likely first fight. Ottilie's ledger: dead at 50; a bow on this cast forecasts 60 to 91, so a line at 50 never comes within ten points of binding. Filed #540: move the ledger to 65, so on the boss-on-fort shot she's at 60 alone and 65 with Teodor's order, and refuses the hard shot unless he tells her; the two signatures interlock. One observation about Recall, not a reopening: rolls are keyed, so a rewind shows you the roll of any attack you have already made. On turn 15 I knew Wren's 72 would land, because I had watched it land in the undone line. I'd have taken it anyway, but the journal should say that I knew. Recall is partly scouting.
The moment I stopped caring: none named.

## 2026-09-30 — Sallow Grange with overwatch (13.17 keep round, issue 501), the cold play — Chat

Seed: 601 on `docs/samples/sallow_grange_overwatch.map` (`overwatch: on`), on main at 87d7ca3. Result: won, seize, on turn 6 of 10, nobody dead, no Recalls. Cold: I had not read the 577 entry before playing.
Tension: 5/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 4/10
Best turn: turn 2, and it was the watch. Pell's Cinder on the fort archer (10 at 91) woke the field. The captain missed, and Wren finished it from 5,2, adjacent, where a bow can't answer. That left Ottilie. Her only strike on the woken field was from 5,5 (soldier-1 at 82 for 6, no counter), and `threat ottilie from 4,4` next door read 33 against 17. So I put her on 3,3, out of every reach, and she watched. Her ring covered 5,3 and 3,5, the two tiles the soldier needed to reach the captain or Teodor. It ended on 3,5, took her shot for 6, and then Teodor's counter left it on 5. Turn 3 she finished it at 82 with no counter.
Notes: Turn 3 cleared the field. Wren and the captain killed archer-2 from 7,2 and 7,4, both adjacent, so no counter and no ring. Pell took the brawler down with 13 at 99, and Ansgar finished it at 77. Turn 4 was staging. Turn 5: Ansgar doubled the hexer from 12,7, beside it and off its ring, which woke the Reeve and left Ansgar on 8. He cantoed to 10,6 behind a wall of Teodor on 12,6 and Wren on 11,6. The captain walked to 14,5. Pell and Ottilie watched from 10,5 and 10,4, because they had nothing to strike. The Reeve stepped to 14,6 and spear-poked Teodor. Turn 6: the captain went round the Reeve to the throne. Against the kill, both clauses, from my chair: clause 1 held; all three of my watches printed `no strike passed up`, and the one that fired was taken on a tile chosen for safety over an 82 strike on a lethal tile, a choice of tile, not a trade of watch against strike. Clause 2 held; I never moved to stay out of a printed ring. Both archers' rings were irrelevant by construction: a bow reaches 2 and only 2, so the tile beside it is the best tile in the game, no counter and no ring. The hexer's ring doesn't cover the four tiles beside it, and Ansgar took one of them for the double anyway. The shieldbearer has no ring, and the gate never mattered. No keep signal: there was no body order at the mouth, because nothing ever needed to stand at 2. Verdict: kill 13.17 as printed. Both chairs have now played the keep round, and both clauses held in both plays; that's the criterion we wrote, and it now holds in six plays on three boards. The reason is structural: the enemy half puts its ring at exactly 2, around units whose tile at 1 is already the best tile to attack from, so a ring only taxes the player's ranged units, and they are exactly the ones who can afford to stand at 3; the player half is a free upgrade of Wait, and something that strictly dominates Wait is a stat, not a decision. The idea worth keeping is "a ring shapes where a foe ends its move" (Code's turn 4 on 577, my turn 2). It could come back as a new section 13 item with a cost that makes it a trade, and only if someone writes that version. Two small findings, moot if the kill stands: `help` on an overwatch map doesn't list `watch`; the printed rings include wall tiles (11,7 and 13,9 around the hexer).
The moment I stopped caring: turn 4. Six units walking east with nothing awake that could reach them.

Both findings confirmed in the code: the `help` text has no `watch` line, and the ring is every tile at distance 2 with terrain unread.

## 2026-09-30 — Cover on the Tollgate (13.19, issue 531), the cold play — Chat

Seed: 113 on `docs/samples/the_tollgate_cover.map` (`cover: on`), on main at 7c505ee. Result: won, seize, on turn 9 of 10, nobody dead, two Recalls spent. Cold: I had not read Code's 113 entry or transcript before playing.
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 8, and it was the cover. The state going in: the boss was on 10 after Wren's double counter on turn 7; Wren was on 2 at 6,2, beside the archer, where a bow can't reach her; Pell was on 6. The only tile Pell could take the boss from was 6,3. From there it was 13 at 99 with no counter, and she crit it for 39. But 6,3 sits in the archer's ring, and the archer's shot on her was 8 at 82 against 6 HP. The captain stepped to 6,4 behind her and took `cover captain pell`. The enemy phase printed `archer-1's strike would have killed pell`, swapped them, and missed the captain. Turn 9: Wren finished the archer and the captain walked through to the throne.
Notes: The Recalls, honestly. Turn 5 (the first): I left Pell where she couldn't reach 6,4 the next turn, so the gate would have been Teodor alone at 6,3 on 14 HP, facing 23 if all landed. Turn 6 (the second): I reordered the gate, Pell's Cinder first from 6,4, then Teodor's lance, so the warden died without countering and Teodor stood at 6,3 on 21 against a 16 ceiling. Every cover decision, with its answer. Turn 4, brigand: Teodor at 6,6 could strike or cover Pell; cover would have put him on her forest tile at range 2, where a lance can't counter, and cost her 87% Cinder counter; I struck. Turn 6, rider: Wren or the captain could cover Pell at 6,4; the swap would have traded her 99% counter for a sword's; I waited. Turn 7: the one unit with a strike, Wren at 6,2, could have covered Teodor or Pell; the swap would have landed the coverer on the tile the boss or the archer was about to kill; it was never close. Turn 8: the captain had nothing to strike from any tile he could reach; the line printed `no strike passed up`. So: one cover taken, it fired, it was by a unit with nothing else to do, and there was no `passed` line. The surprises: the crit; the boss's Toll Axe reaching 7,4 through the wall, which `threat` showed, so it was fair; and turn 7, where the boss chose Wren over nothing and her double counter put it on 10. Verdict: kill 13.19 by the letter; both plays land on the same clause. The decision is real, but it sits on the wrong unit: turn 8's choice was Pell's, cover is what let a 6-HP adept take the one lethal tile the boss could be killed from, and the coverer's side of it was free. Wherever the coverer did have a strike, cover was dominated for a structural reason: the swap drops the coverer onto the ally's tile at the ally's range, and on every board we've played that tile is either one where its weapon can't counter or the tile the enemy is about to kill. So the pattern holds: a free body behind the striker, taken in place of Wait, the same failure as 13.17's player half.
The moment I stopped caring: turns 1 to 3. The woods pair are `hold` and never move, so the approach was walking.
