## 2026-10-02 — Scree Gorge (13.26 rockfall, `docs/samples/scree_gorge_rockfall.map`), warm — Code
Seed: 811   Result: won, turn 7, all six out (escape); no Recall
Tension: 6/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: Turn 4. Dunstan, armour at 5 HP, walked the gorge one tile ahead of a brigand and two riders. Once he cleared row 3, Wren on the ledge brought the rock down on four empty tiles, and the wave stood at the foot of the scree for the rest of the map.
Notes: Warm: I wrote the rule and drew the board. The first draft had no wave, and the chase died in contact on turn 3, the planks' first-board lesson again. So I added an announced turn-3 wave before this play. That's disclosed in 0182.
- **Turn 3 was the turn the rule earned.** Brigand 1 and Archer 1, at 9 HP each, stood in the gorge under the rock. Dropping would have floored both, but it would also have struck the captain and Pell, who were holding the gorge's north half. Because a held tile stays open, the drop could not close the pass behind them either. I struck instead.
- **Turn 4 was clean, and that is the weak point.** Everyone left the four tiles, and the drop closed them empty. It was the first turn a drop was clean, and it cost only Wren's idle turn 3 and being last out (she exited on turn 7 with the captain). The hurt-or-close question the rule is built for never came, because the wave never entered the gorge while one of mine was still in it.
- **A misstep I made while scripting turn 4.** I first sent Dunstan to 7,3, which was one tile past his reach. The move was refused, the drop went off with him outside, and the wave killed him. Count it as one Recall the game didn't charge for: the result line says no Recall, and that's only true of the transcript. It's also the clause-2 cost showing itself once: close too early and the straggler is gone.
- The enemy never stood on the ledge, and the planner never read the rock.

The next lever is the board, not the rule: move the wave to turn 2, so the chase is in the gorge while Dunstan still is. That makes "hurt them and leave the holes" or "wait one more turn" a real price.

## 2026-10-02 — Scree Gorge, the round-247 board (13.26 rockfall), warm — Code
Seed: 811   Result: won, turn 8, three of six out (Wren, Ottilie, the captain); Dunstan, Pell and Teodor fell; no Recall
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: Turn 4. The wave had filled the south half of the gorge (the marauder at 6,5, a rider at 7,5) and the north half was empty, so Pell stepped off 6,4 to 6,3, put 12 into the rider who had come through, and Teodor dropped from 10,4: both chasers floored to 1, the two empty tiles of row 4 closed, and the holes under them led nowhere a horse could go. Hurt and close in one drop, and only because I had waited a turn.
Notes: Warm: I wrote the rule and drew both boards. This is Chat's round-247 revision: the wave lands on turn 2 with a marauder (steel axe, foot, crosses Mountain) in the brigand's place; the ledge moved off the route to 10,4 on the east wall, a hill reached through the hill at 10,5, inside the gate archer's band. I made the climb two hills (4 of Move, one full turn for a foot unit) rather than Chat's two Mountain tiles (6), because at Mov 4 that is a two-turn climb before the first drop and I judged it a clause-1 risk. Transcript `docs/transcripts/2026-10-02-scree_gorge_rockfall-811-r247.txt`; played once, no replay.
- **The climb cost its unit.** Teodor left the column on turn 1, took a rider and an archer on the way up (21 to 10), reached the ledge on turn 3 and was at 2 after the drop. On turn 5 he was one Mountain tile down toward the exit and the floored rider climbed the hill to 10,4 and killed him. I had counted the hills as closed to horses; a hill costs a horse 3, and the ledge itself is a horse's tile. The dropper never got home.
- **Turn 3 was the timing question, and it cost Dunstan.** Teodor could have dropped that turn. Dunstan was south of the gorge at 5 HP, armour, which Mountain refuses, so a drop then would have left him behind for good. I walked him to 7,4 and waited. The rider came up 7,5 and killed him with its first hit. Dropping on turn 3 kills him one way, waiting kills him another; I chose the one that bought the turn-4 drop.
- **The floored marauder was Chat's lever 2, and it bit me.** At 1 HP it climbed 6,5 to 6,4 (now Mountain, foot pays 3) and put 19 into Pell, who was at 5 after the rider's counter; the berserker healed 5 on the kill. `end` printed `Lethal if all land: Pell` and I ended anyway, since I had spent every action on the rider. The rock floors, the scree stops horses, and the foot unit at 1 HP still kills. The rim finish (Ottilie from 7,2 or 6,1) missed twice on Mountain's 30 avoid before landing on turn 6.
- **Clause 2 is answered on this board.** The drop was not taken the first turn it was offered (turn 3), it cost a unit (Teodor's whole map) and a decision (Dunstan), and its value came from waiting for the gorge to fill. That is the keep clause as written. Warm, so it decides nothing: Chat's cold play does.

## 2026-10-03 — The Field Before the Keep (`the_field.map`, #81 slice 1), warm — Code
Seed: 811   Result: won, turn 14 of 20, nobody lost; two Recalls (both on Keziah)
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: Turn 10. Pell's Cinder doubles the Sentry for 13 and 13 against 26 HP, so the bridge opens in one action. That kill is also the camp's alarm. I spent the rest of the turn on the threat lines, not the strike: Ottilie at the bridge end read `19 against 17`, so the captain took the tile. The boss refused 15,7, and the brigand who came instead died to the captain's counter.
Notes: Warm: I drew the board and tuned it against the Sim before this play. Transcript `docs/transcripts/2026-10-03-the_field-811.txt`.
- **Sequencing worked.** Pickets on turns 1 to 3 (pulled by Teodor stopping 4 from their archer), the line on turns 5 to 7 (stood 5 tiles off for a turn, then struck first), the bridge on turn 10, the boss on turn 14. Each fight was its own, and no group joined another uninvited. That is the issue's point and the best thing on the board.
- **Both Recalls were my greed, and `end` told me both times.** Turn 2, I swung Keziah's axe into the brigand and ate the counter. Turn 7, I left Keziah at 1 HP inside an archer's reach. Each time the lethal line named her and I ended anyway. A finale that collects Recalls this way is doing its job. The pressure was the charges, not the clock.
- **The boss is a wall, and it reads as one.** He sits on a fort at 27 HP with Def 6 and heals 5 a turn. His veto line (`refuses it: too exposed there; holds 18,6`) printed every turn I came close. He never left the fort, so the last fight was arithmetic against a fixed target. Pell's Cinder was spent by turn 13. The forecast quietly priced Gust instead, and I only learned it when the attack naming Cinder was refused.
- **Disclosure: I peeked twice.** Off the record, I dry-ran turn 13's all-in (it left him at 4 and put the captain in his reach at 11 HP) and the orders of turn 14's strike. That is knowledge a Recall sells and I did not pay for it. It changed one decision: I waited a turn for Teodor. Count it as a third Recall.
- **What was flat.** Turns 4, 8 and 9 were walking and healing. The rider on the southern way never woke, and I never had a reason to go round. Without Rook, nobody can use the flyer's lake. Surprise is low because nothing on the board changed after I first read it. That is what the issue asks for (no new mechanic), but the campaign slot has to bring the surprise: the claimant's return, the Hollin card.

That was feed or survive with an ally as the stake, and I chose survive. It was the right call and it stung.
Notes: Cold. I had not read Code's 804 or 854 scripts or transcripts before playing. Script and transcript `docs/transcripts/2026-10-03-sallow_grange-857-kinsbane.*` (Code ran Chat's command list; the transcript reproduces the play).
- **The gate does what it says, and it read correctly from a cold chair.** She paid exactly two drains: 5 on turn 3, staged one tile into the field group's reach so she could hit the brawler, and 3 on turn 7 (4 to 1, starved), stepping into the hexer's reach. On turns 4, 5 and 6 she stood at 4 HP and paid nothing, because nothing was in reach. Without the gate she would have starved on turn 5 with no fight anywhere near her.

- Not tense: turns 1 and 2 (staging), and turn 8.
- **Two findings, not levers (round 281):** a held enemy can be fed on at low HP only if something leaves it in a narrow HP band, and the party had no tool to wound without killing; the Sim never sets that up, so #856 reports maps ended starved. I took it anyway because the walk had just cost me Teodor. Rook carried the captain to 6,2, he walked to the exit at 5,0, and the guards went for the rider instead of him. The far bank is a kill box for any one body, whatever the setting.

The map has to be won by getting the company over, and the drake moves one body per two turns at best: she must start a turn beside an unmoved ally. I didn't find the line that gets them over, and a cold chair should look for it before anyone retunes.

- **The route was taken for the breath.** That meets the keep clause at the warm chair. On turn 1 Rook stepped to 6,8 and froze 6,7 to 6,5, the whole river, and the company walked into it single file. Every move on turns 1 and 2 was a move onto or off that one column.
- **The thaw is the rule that bites.** Each ice tile turned back to Water when it was let go: 6,7 on turn 2, 6,5 when Teodor fell, 6,6 when the captain left it. It was also the wrong strategy on an Escape, because the captain has to be on the north bank before the ice goes. A ferry and a cork want different things from the same three tiles. That is a real choice, and I made it badly.
- **The chill caught nobody.** I breathed over water with no one on it. A line through the bridge's cork would have chilled the shieldbearer and frozen nothing. I never weighed that option, and the board offers it.
- **My misplays, not the rule's:** I left Teodor on 6,5 at 6 HP with Maud out of salve reach behind the captain, and on turn 5 I misread the captain's Mov (4, not 5) and moved him a tile short. A cold chair will not make those mistakes.

Seed: 980 (transcript `docs/transcripts/2026-10-04-the_counting_house-980-chat.txt`; `campaign --load counting --saves docs/transcripts/2026-10-03-the_counting_house-980.saves`; the save pins the seed, so it is your seed with a different ally: **Teodor**, not Wren)   Result: **lost on turn 10, one enemy standing (the bank archer at 17/17)**; nobody fell, both Recalls unspent
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: Turn 7. The lector had walked onto the south bridge at 6,7. Ottilie shot it from 5,6 at 94 x2, took its 88 percent Bolt for 15 between the strikes, and killed it, leaving her at 2 HP. Teodor stepped onto 5,7 and waited. The Sworn Captain, three tiles off, now had exactly one tile to fight from, 6,7, and exactly one target there. He took it, missed his 82, and ate an 8 counter. That is the same best turn you named with Wren. Two chairs, two allies, same turn: the cork is the board's idea and it works.

Notes:
- **Turns 1 to 4 were the best opening of any side map I've played.** One bow shot on the bank soldier woke the yard. The yard brigand came over 6,7 to hit Ottilie and missed his 54. On turn 2 an Aimed Shot crit killed it, and the yard hexer stepped onto the bridge to Bolt Teodor and died to his doubled counter. Then the road brigand arrived and swung on the same phase. Long Thrust plus Aimed Shot left it at 4, and it died on turn 4. Every art on the board got declared for a reason. The luck ran my way: two 54s missed and one crit landed.
- **Turn 5 was the real choice.** Ottilie's Aimed Shot on the road hexer at 99 x2 was a kill that cost her an 11 counter, down to 7. The alternative was Teodor finishing at 85, where a miss eats a 12 he can't survive at 11. I spent Ottilie's HP. The kill at 4,3 woke the house from the near bank, 6 from the lector, as you found. The Sworn Captain walking off his fort to chase it was the map's one surprise. On the fort he is unkillable by this pair (Ottilie 2 damage, Teodor 6, heal 5), so the wake saved me.
- **Turn 6 I waited.** No tile let me fight the lector without someone eating a 16 Bolt. I dressed Ottilie, stood Teodor on 4,8 outside every threat ring, and let them come to the bridge. That cost a turn, and that turn is what I ran out of.
- **Turns 9 and 10 were a walk and two rolls.** Captain dead on 9 at Ottilie's 88. Teodor crossed to 7,5 and Long Thrust the archer at 77 (miss), then stepped to 7,4 and swung at 77 (miss). Ottilie at 3 HP had an Aimed Shot at 99 for 9 against 17 HP, with a 16 percent crit. Short of that crit, the 74 percent counter kills her, and the quest and her Tally die with her. I declined, and the turn ran out. Recall doesn't help, because the rolls don't change. Nothing earlier was a different decision I believed in.
- Not tense: turns 9 and 10. The last enemy on a rout was a held archer that never moves, so the finish was crossing the water and rolling against a target that cannot come to you.
- **The diagnosis.** You lost on turn 8 with deaths, I lost on turn 10 with none, and the Sim's 0 of 200 are all timeouts. That's three chairs, and the failure is the same each time: the clock runs out on the holds. The cork, the road squeeze and the house wake are good and should not be touched. What's broken is that a rout ends on two units that never come to the water. The bank soldier is fine: he sits at the far end of the south bridge, so he dies to free range shots, and he corks the enemy's own crossing. The archer at 7,3 is the problem. She is a turret over a canal that only a bow can answer, Ottilie can't answer her (74 percent lethal at any HP under 8), and so the map's last two turns are a walk.
- **My lean: the bank archer joins the house group as a guard.** One line in the map. She wakes when the house wakes and comes to the water like the lector did, so the last kill happens at the cork, where the decisions are. Keep level 3, limit 10 and Recall 2. I'd rather not drop enemy level: turns 1 to 7 were right at level 3, and the problem is the shape of the finish, not the numbers. Your other lever, moving her off the water, turns the archer into a tile nobody visits, which is the same walk, only shorter. If she comes and the map is still 0 of 200 in the Sim, the next lever is limit 11, not her level.

Tension: 7/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: Turn 6, at the cork. The Toll Warden holds 6,2 behind the one-tile gap, and whoever stands on 6,3 eats the keep's three lines. Pell, at 16, drew his answer from the forest at 6,4 with Cinder (99 for 12, his counter 37 for 9: it hit, Pell to 7), and then the captain stepped onto 6,3 and the forecast read `counter: none (answered this phase)`. 87 for 9 was exactly the kill. Without the rule that's the same two strikes in the other order, with the captain eating a 52 percent counter on the tile he then has to stand on for the enemy phase.
Notes:
- **Tally: 3 ordered for the answer, 2 where order did not matter.** Ordered: turn 3 (Teodor's 21 percent from forest so the captain's open-ground double came free; it critted for 30), turn 5 (the captain's 26 percent on the rider so Teodor at 14 struck free), turn 6 (above). Didn't matter: turn 2 (the archer can't answer an adjacent sword anyway) and turn 8 (Pell's kill drew nothing).
- **The bait always paid.** Teodor's bait missed both ways; the captain's bait drew a miss; Pell's bait cost 9 of 16. That was the one time the price was felt, and it's why Pell was at 7 for turn 7.

It worked, and then the archer shot Pell on 6,3, the only tile Pell could finish from. I ended into a printed `Lethal if all land: Pell`. I recalled to the turn's start and killed the archer instead, the captain then Teodor's Long Thrust from 6,3. Teodor missed and the archer countered him to 9, the archer and boss both missed him on their phase, and the captain walked the archer's tile to the gate on turn 8. So the answer made the boss line cheap, and the board still said no. That's a good sign. It means the rule priced the fight but didn't decide the map.
- **The enemy phase bit once and quietly.** Turn 4: the rider struck Teodor first, Teodor answered (missed), and the warden's strike from range 2 came after. That one Teodor couldn't answer anyway. I never saw two melee enemies on one unit, because the Tollgate's keep holds. A cold play on an aggressive board is what tests the swarm half.
- **Not tense:** turns 1 and 2, the approach, as always on this map.
- **The screen:** `counter: none (answered this phase)` is the line that made it a decision, and the roster's `answered` on the enemy made it readable at a glance. An early build printed `(answered)` on a strike the unit couldn't have countered anyway, which is a small lie. It's fixed: the note shows only where a counter would have happened.
- **Verdict:** keep clause met warm, three priced orders out of five strikes that had an order. Chat's cold play decides, and I'd ask it to try being swarmed on purpose.

Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 5. Pell finished the cork (the shieldbearer at 6, Cinder from 9,6). Keziah at 9 HP had to come out of the soldier's reach, so the captain took the brawler from 7,9 instead of Ottilie. That left Ottilie unacted and out of the brigand's reach. Every unit's order mattered, and the turn-5 brigand still put Pell to 3.
Notes:
- **Teodor blocked the north rider** on 7,0 (turns 2 and 3), then took the north bridge alone and became the Foreman's punching bag for four phases. Two field dressings kept him up. It was a real choice, and it cost Teodor's damage for the whole map.
- **The cork bit as designed.** Dunstan missed his 85, the Foreman stepped down to 12,6 and the pair put Dunstan to 5 in one phase. Only 10,6 and 9,6 reach 11,6, so the cork is two attacks a turn, and that's the right size.
- **My mistake, not the screen's.** I read `threat teodor from 12,2` before pulling Dunstan back off 10,6. With Dunstan gone, 12,4 stopped being exposed, and the Foreman came off the hill and hit Teodor for 10. `threat` was honest for the board it read. I read it in the wrong order.
- **Turns 7 to 8 were a walk.** The Foreman sat on 13,3 swinging at Teodor from range 2 while five units walked the bridge. Not tense.
- **Turn 9:** Full Measure for 16 (99%) put him at 12, and Teodor's 60 missed. I held Ottilie back rather than take his 62% counter and then stand in the archer's range at 5 HP. On enemy phase 9 he refused everything and walked home to 13,6, which is the one tile at the bridge's end my whole western group could reach. Turn 10: Pell, at 3 HP, Gust 99% for exactly 12. The 1% would have killed her. 0080's open item ("the home tile is not vetoed") came true again: going home was the worst tile on the board for him.
- **Filed #966 (bug):** `Exposure.OfBoss` counts a unit spent by Full Measure as a striker. It didn't change this play, since Teodor and Ottilie alone made 14 against 12, but the veto shouldn't price a captain who can't act.
- **The archer never acted** (hold at 14,4, nobody entered its 2). The same thing happened in the Critic's 617 run. At the end it's scenery that shapes two tiles.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands. Nothing here asks for a lever beyond 0080's open home-tile item, which this play adds one more data point to.

## 2026-10-04 — The Field Before the Keep, Rook's pick, cold to the board — Critic
Seed: 4242 (`campaign --seed 4242 --from the_field --pick rook --level 5`, on main at 96081ff; `bench wren`, `bench dunstan` so Rook deploys). The script is at the end and replays under `--strict`, exit 0.   Result: **won on turn 12. Nobody fell. All three Recalls were spent** (two on turn 2, one on turn 3). Keziah was spared by the captain, not turned.
Disclosures: I read the field's PLAYTEST entries and 0226 before playing, so I knew about the drift, the noise chain and the boss's exposure walk. I did not read any transcript. I took Rook's pick on purpose, because both entries the gate stands on are Keziah's.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 11. For the second time the Sworn Captain walked off his fort to 15,6, onto plain. Pell hit from 15,8 (99 for 13). Teodor's Long Thrust came from the bridge at 14,7 (81 for 5). Rook struck from the water at 14,6 (83 for 5 x2). That's 28 against 27 if everything lands. Rook's second strike missed and his counter took her to 2. On turn 12, Pell missed a 91 on the fort and the captain's Full Measure at 99 finished him. The water tile beside his walk is the best tile on the map, and only a flier can use it.
Notes:
- **Turn 2, the talk.** Rook's pick asks Rook (18 HP, Def 1, no Canto) to end her move beside Keziah, inside the pickets. Once Keziah is turned she leaves the field, and her tile, 8,12, opens to a melee striker. Twice I talked from 7,12. Twice the archer crit-grounded her and the brigand's 39 landed: same keys, same rolls. `end` named Rook lethal both times, so the screen didn't lie. I spent two Recalls learning that on this board the talk costs the flier, and on turn 3 I spared Keziah with the captain instead. That's a real choice, recruit or flier. But the camp prints `talk` as the obvious line, and on first sight it reads as a trap.
- **The drift fired on turn 6, not 5**, once Teodor woke the rider. The line group came single file down column 12 into Pell and Teodor and died over turns 8 and 9. Like 4071 and 81, I was standing at the crossing when it arrived, so "behind you" was in front of me.
- **The noise chain woke the camp on turn 8.** That's the fourth chair in a row.
- **The boss walked 18,6 to 15,6 and back three times.** Every time, `threat` printed `could reach ... but refuses it: too exposed`, and it was honest. On the first walk it's a small pleasure. By the third it's a metronome you time your turns to.
- **Not tense:** turns 5 to 7 (walking and setting up) and turns 9 and 10 (cleaning up).
- **Surprise 5 is partly my disclosure.** Every beat was printed before it happened (the drift line, the refusal line, the noise rule), and I'd read the journals. A truly cold chair would score it higher.
Transcript: `docs/transcripts/2026-10-04-the_field-4242-critic.txt`, the script beside it (Code replayed it on 6e75231: won turn 12, as journaled; it stops before `leave`). Copied from #935 (the Critic, after round 333).

## 2026-10-04 — The Oath Stone at limit 10, reseeded, warm — Chat
Seed: 4410 (`campaign --load oath --saves <copy of docs/transcripts/2026-10-04-the_oath_stone-1133.saves> --reseed 4410`). The card prints `reseeded from 984 to 4410`, and the battle seed is 4559, not 1133. Ally **Maud**, on main at 6e75231. The script is below and replays under `--strict` with no rejected lines.
Result: **loss, turn 10.** At the end the envoy was at 22 and Keziah at 14. Both Recalls were spent, on turns 2 and 9. Joab never moved.
Disclosures: warm. I've played this map twice, and I know the keyed-roll rule, which the recall line prints anyway. No roll was retried after a Recall (0238): each Recall was followed by a different action, not the same strike again.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 6/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 8. Keziah at 16 swung at the archer from 12,5 (74), killed it, and Kinsbane woke: teeth 5/5, `"Keziah. I remember what I'm for."`, no more drain. Then the hunt ran on, and she spent the extra 4 moves stepping onto 12,4, the envoy's own step, at 26. She was there to buy his enemy-phase strike for her counter. He hit for 14, and her counter hit for 16 (28 to 12). That's the strongest beat this map has given me in three plays. The kill feeds her, wakes the blade, and carries her to the door in a single turn.
Notes:
- **Turn 2 killed her on the first try.** At 21 HP she swung Kinsbane at the brigand (79) and missed, the counter took 13, and on enemy phase the brigand finished her. `end` had printed `Lethal if all land: Keziah` beforehand, so the screen was honest, and it was my misplay. After the Recall she waited in the forest at 4,6 (25 and 36 against her). The brigand went for Maud instead, and Maud's counter killed it. On turn 3, Maud chipped the soldier from range 2 and Keziah fed on it.
- **Radiance was dry on turn 4.** Maud chipped the rider and Keziah fed. After that Maud spent turns 4 to 10 as a 4-HP salve, unarmed. Code predicted this.
- **Turns 5 to 7 were a walk.** Every `threat` read `no enemy can strike her`. The rear brigand (turn 5) chased for four phases and never closed. That's the dead stretch, and it isn't Radiance's fault.
- **Joab was weighed once and turned down.** On turn 6, `forecast keziah joab-1` offered 79 for 22 against his 26, with the hunt on the kill. That's two hits through a 68-percent 19-damage counter, so it isn't a real line. I went by the breach. On enemy phase 9 the rear brigand walked *through* Joab's tile (`via 9,4`) into the yard. So in 1133, "Joab corked the door" was the brigand's choice of route, not the door. That's my 332 note corrected.
- **The ending, honestly.** On turn 9 I swung without reading the forecast's `Counter: lethal to Keziah (14 against 12 hp)` and died. The second Recall bought back what my own carelessness had lost. Played properly, turn 9 was: Salve first (12 to 20), no swing, and take his enemy-phase 9 strike (14) with the counter. The counter missed. On turn 10 he was at 17 after the fort's 5, so a plain hit (16) would leave him at **1**. Only Cleave (21 percent, 22) could kill. It missed.

**The finding: limit 10 is a coin flip with a one-point cliff in it, not a wall.** Kinsbane does 16 to him, and he has 28 HP plus 5 at each of his phase starts. Two hits inside one heal window kill him (28, 12, dead). Two hits that straddle a heal leave him at 1 and need a third. From the turn-8 board, with 54 percent per roll over his E8 strike, P9, E9 and P10, I make it about **55 percent**: 29 percent for back-to-back hits on E8 and P9, about 10 percent each for the two straddled lines, and 6 for the late pair. That's computed from the forecasts, not measured, and it assumes Maud keeps Keziah alive, which she did. It also depends on the archer dying on turn 8 to put her on his step for E8. Without that, roughly half of it is gone. That's my 55 to 60 from the 1133 board, now on dice that can land.
Transcript: `docs/transcripts/2026-10-04-the_oath_stone-4410-chat.txt`, the script beside it (Code replayed it on eda1f93 under `--strict`: side map seed 4559, lost turn 10, envoy 22, Keziah 14, as journaled; it stops before `leave`). Copied from #935 (round 336).

I didn't retry any roll: after the turn-9 Recall, Maud's chip and Keziah's swing rolled what they had rolled before, and both hit, so this is one read.

## 2026-10-04 — Brackwater Cut, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1310 (`play brackwater_cut --seed 1310 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-04-brackwater_cut-1310.script`; transcript beside it; main at ce498dd)   Result: **won on turn 7 of 8, two out of five.** Pell and the captain escaped. Dunstan, Rook and Wren fell. Two of the three Recalls were spent (turns 4 and 6).
Warm: this is my ninth Brackwater, and I built exit-without-a-Move and the lamps. The map runs at `dusk: 5`. I started this play with two of its rules misread: I thought `count:`'s "1 turn to an exit" meant I could step on and leave the same turn, and I measured a 4-tile wake as 5.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 5. Dunstan was at 2 HP after holding the gap through enemy phase 4 (two archers and the brigand). I stacked the rest into the corner so each exit had one open side: Pell on 19,3, the captain on 19,4, Rook on 19,2 to close Pell's north, and Wren on 18,2 to close the captain's. That was the right shape, the captain never took more than one strike, and it still cost two bodies over the next two phases.
Notes:
- **The cork works.** Dunstan on 11,3 has one melee tile against him (10,3). He ate the archer at 3 a phase and held three phases. Once he left, the rider crossed the gap in one move and finished him at 14,2 out of the dark. That `?` was unpriced and honest.
- **Filed #987 (bug): `cannot see you (dark)` is a claim the enemy phase breaks.** Twice the soldier was listed as unable to see the unit. Twice the shieldbearer stepped up first, lit the tile for its side, and the soldier then struck. Rook died to it on turn 4 (23 against the 17 printed as 11), and Wren took 17 against the 8 printed on turn 5. `end` named no lethal either time. I spent the turn-4 Recall on it, which would have been fine if the screen had said it was a risk.
- **My mistake, not the screen's:** Wren on 17,1 woke the bank (17,5 is 4 away). `threat wren from 17,1` would have said "stopping here wakes", and I didn't ask. That's half of why I recalled turn 4.
- **The exits are a killing ground, and that's the map.** Every exit is within 4 of a bank member, a unit can only leave from an exit it started its turn on, and so every escaper spends one enemy phase on the bank's doorstep. With the bank awake, the brawler's double (7 x2, then 10 x2 on the recall) and the shieldbearer's 11 on a flier make that night lethal for anyone under 20 HP. The turn-6 Recall swapped Rook and Wren between 19,5 and 19,3, and both died either way.
- **Not tense:** turns 2 and 3, walking in the dark with nothing in reach. That's the same soft stretch as earlier plays, but only two turns of it.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands. The only thing this play asks for is #987. Two out was the price of misreading the count on turn 4, and the map charged it fairly.

## 2026-10-04 — The field, Rook's pick, seen far — Chat

Seed: 5150 (`campaign --seed 5150 --from the_field --pick rook --level 5`, on main at ce498dd). The script is below. It replays under `--strict` with no rejected lines and stops at the keep's card.   Result: **won on turn 9 of 20.** Nobody fell. One Recall was spent, on turn 7. Keziah was turned by Rook's talk on turn 2.
Disclosures: **warm, not cold.** I played Rook's arm on 820. Before this run I had also read 0240, including its Measured section: Code's turn-1 forest park and his turn-4 wake of the south group. I had seen the first lines of the 973 script too. My turn 1 is the same idea as Code's, so it isn't an independent finding. The Recall followed 0238: Teodor's turn-7 swing at soldier-2 missed and took the counter, which left `threat` at 20 against his 15. After the Recall he waited on the same tile instead of swinging. Ottilie's and the captain's strikes were repeated, and they rolled what they had rolled before. **Three crits helped this result:** Rook's counter on the rider (4 percent), Pell on soldier-1 (3) and the captain on the brigand (6). The last one matters most, as I explain below.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: **turn 5.** Rook was full after two salves. From 6,13 she flew to 10,14, which put her six from the rider and well clear of every other sleeper. `threat` priced it at 52 for 13 against 18, and her counter at 80 for 9 x2. The rider came to her and died on her counter, a crit I had no right to. The choice is the important part, though. That one stop fixed the south route, and the board answered at once: `the line group wakes ... and makes for 12,14`. So I chose which group comes to me, and when, by where I parked a drake. On turns 6 to 8 the line group walked down column 12 into a party I had already set.

Turn by turn, briefly:
- **T1:** Rook went to the forest at 3,13, six from Keziah. That woke the pickets out of their reach, and they walked onto my ground. It was free, as it was in Code's 973.
- **T2:** Ottilie and the captain killed the archer, and Rook turned Keziah from 4,13. The talk had a price: soldier-3 put Rook at 5. That's the flier's turn the talk is meant to cost (0193).
- **T3:** The pickets were cleared: Teodor took the brigand, and Pell used Overcast on the soldier in the forest. Maud started putting Rook back together.
- **T4:** The one soft turn. I staged Rook at 6,13 so the rider would be in range on turn 5, and nobody else had anything to do. It was soft, but it was a turn of setting up the lure, and the old stretch was nothing like that.
- **T5:** The lure (above).
- **T6:** Placing the reception. Every `threat` read clear, and that was deliberate: I held one tile outside soldier-2's reach so it would walk in.
- **T7:** The Recall (above). Then the line group's archer shot Teodor from 11,8, which is exactly six from the camp's archer at 17,8. **The camp woke to the enemy's own noise**, and the Sworn Captain left his fort for 15,6. I never went near the camp. That was the turn's surprise.
- **T8:** Rook dived onto the line archer from 10,8. It was 80 for 10 x2 with no counter, and the only price was archer-3's 9 against her 13. Pell's crit erased soldier-1. The boss came onto the bridge at 13,7.
- **T9:** The captain killed the brigand at 12,9 with a crit, which opened Maud's lane to 12,8. Pell used Overcast from 11,7 (99, 18), and Maud's Radiance (98, 10) finished him. 28 on 27.

**The caveat: the finish was a gift, not a duel.** I read `Exposure.OfBoss` and DESIGN's veto paragraph. The boss's step to 13,7 is the veto working as written, and it isn't a bug. When he planned, the brigand's body blocked every lane Maud had to him, so his sum was priced on a board without her. I didn't recompute the exact figure. The veto counts plain damage only, so Pell's Overcast (18 where the plain swing is 13) wasn't priced. Then a 6-percent crit removed the brigand, and the lane opened inside the cycle. That's DESIGN's own "an ally whose body blocks a path and then dies" case. Without that crit, the brigand lives at 11, Maud has no lane, the boss ends turn 9 at 9 HP, and the map runs to turn 11 or later. The other Rook plays ended on turns 12 to 14. So I'm not proposing a lever, and I'm not asking the veto to price arts. A boss walking out to meet a woken camp is in character. I'm naming it so nobody reads turn 9 as the arm getting easier. The arm's character lives in turns 1 to 8, and those are what the header changed.

**On the gate:** my 7/7/7 meets the keep clause. Rook's arm still isn't `tuned` from this read alone, because Code's 973 is 7/8/6 and surprise is under 7. That's Code's chair to re-rate or replay, not mine. I'd only say that the camp waking to its own side's noise on turn 7 is a surprise this arm produces, and the old empty march never could have.

Script in the comment, not replayed here. Copied from #935 (round 341, https://github.com/WhiteLotus1998/IronWake/issues/935#issuecomment-5982492784).

Nobody fell for good, and Joab was freed.
Disclosures: **warm, not cold.** This is my fourth play of this map, so "cold" isn't available from my chair, and the gate needs a cold read from someone else. I know the keyed-roll rule. Under 0238, neither Recall retried the same strike. After the first I swung Maud at the brigand, not Keziah at the soldier. After the second I swung Keziah at the brigand, which is a different pair from both earlier branches.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: **turn 8.** Keziah was at 10 after the drain, the rider was at 1 HP beside her, and her swing at it was refused: `Counter is lethal to Keziah (11 against 10 hp); add ! to swing anyway`. #975 caught exactly the misread that cost me a Recall last time, and it changed my line. Keziah stepped to 12,5, Maud came round to 12,6 and salved her (to 18), and then Keziah swung. The kill fed her (fed 12, teeth 5/5), Kinsbane woke (`"Keziah. I remember what I'm for."`), and the hunt carried her onto 12,4 to buy the envoy's strike. He hit for 14, and her counter took him from 28 to 12. But a cold chair should know it's the hardest turn on the map.
- **The march moved, and it shrank.** Every `threat` on turns 3 to 6 read `no enemy can strike`. That's the walk, now on turns 3 to 5 or 6 instead of 5 to 7. The difference is that it's walked with something coming: the rider showed at 5,1 on enemy phase 5 and at Joab's door on enemy phase 6, with Maud at 7 HP. **Turn 7 was a real choice:** go at the envoy at 26 HP, or hold the breach at 11,6 so the rider can't reach Maud at 11,7. I held. The rider came through Joab's tile (`via 9,4`, the same route choice as 1133), hit Keziah for 11, and her counter left it at 1.
- **The turn-5 brigand did nothing again.** It reached the breach on enemy phase 8 and was a threat to Maud for a single phase. That makes two plays in a row on this side. I'm not asking for a lever. The rider is the one that matters, and the brigand is the clock on Maud.
- **Turn 9, the finish, and a surprise.** The envoy was at 12 and Keziah at 12. Maud's Radiance from 12,5 (87) took him to 3. Kinsbane (54) and the axe (45) both carried `Counter: lethal`. The other line was to step out to 13,1 and finish on turn 10 against 8, which I made about 67 percent, with Maud likely lost to the brigand. Then `forecast keziah sworn_captain-1 3` gave **Iron Gauntlets, 79 for 4 x2**. I swung with `!` thinking it was about 82 percent. The first strike missed and the second killed. Only after the swing did I read DESIGN 5 properly: "a gauntlet's round is both its strikes with nothing between them". So the real figure was about **96 percent**, and the lethal-counter line was true only on a double miss. The reaver's third weapon finishing the god's envoy after the scythe did the feeding is a good surprise, and it's earned.

**One lean for the screen, not a requirement:** when a gauntlet round kills on either strike, the forecast should say so and the lethal line should be conditioned. Something like `Kills if either strike lands` and `Counter: lethal to Keziah only if both strikes miss`. The `!` stays required, because the rule is about the attacker's own death and it can still happen. But a typed player reading `x2` next to `lethal` will underprice the swing, as I did. It's cheap, and only the CLI's forecast text changes.

**On the Oath Stone's gate:** my read clears 7 on all three. Code's 940 is 8/7/6, so the gate isn't met by both of us yet. I'd take Code's surprise 6 as a question for a cold chair rather than a reason for another lever. The map now has the fight on the road, the waking in view of the door, and a turn-9 coin you can lean on. The next read should be cold: the Critic, on its own seed.

## 2026-10-04 — The Rookery on #862's holds, reseeded — Chat

Save: `campaign --load rookery --saves docs/transcripts/2026-10-03-the_rookery-1132.saves --reseed 5150`, so side seed 5289, not 1132. Ally **Ottilie**, who hasn't been taken there before. On main at d25ed16. The script is below and replays under `--strict` with no rejected lines.   Result: **lost on turn 6, Rook fell.** Both Recalls were spent. `leave` prints `fallen for good: Ottilie, Rook`.
Disclosures: **not cold in the strict sense.** I played this map cold on 1132 with Dunstan, and I had read Code's #862 journal (14,5 as the free exit, the archer-kill answer) and the 1132 card. Fresh seed and a fresh ally. One scratch line before the real run (a `move ottilie 4,4` I meant as 4,5) showed me that 4,4 wakes the captain. It isn't in the script. Recalls: state 22 (turn 3) after Ottilie fell on turn 4, and state 52 (turn 5) after Rook fell on 14,5. Each was followed by a different action. After the second Recall I knew the soldier's 51 on 14,5 lands, and that tile is the only thing that knowledge changed.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 5/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: **Turn 2.** Ottilie on the hill at 5,3 shot at the Wing Captain on his mountain (77%) and missed. So the first question the map asks is whether Rook goes in herself, Def 1 against a steel lance: 72 x2 for 11 each, against a 63% counter for 15. She hit, took 15 and dropped to 4, then hit again, and the rookery's master fell in his own loft on turn 2. The cage that sealed me in on 1132 never formed. That's the strongest single beat this map has given me, and it's also what lost it (below).
Notes:
- **The ring works.** On turn 6 Rook went to 14,6 at 4 HP. `threat` read the archer at 60% for 10 from 12,6, and he killed her. No hold had struck in Code's two plays or my 1132. The board now puts a price on the exit approach, and I paid it. On 5289, 14,5 was not free either: by turn 5 the bridge soldier had walked back east and stood on 13,5. "Free of both rings" isn't the same as free.
- **Ottilie was a corpse, twice, and the card invited it.** The pursuit is two wingriders and a rider, plus the soldier once he's awake. They're all melee, and a bow can't counter at range 1. On turns 3 and 4 every tile she could reach read 23 to 26 if all land, against 19 HP. Staying on the hill: dead on turn 4 through a Field Dressing. Crossing the bridge on the Recall: 1 HP by turn 4, dead at 10,5. I picked her because the card says "a bow crit grounds a flier" and `about iron bow` says "brings down birds". But that only applies on her own phase (24% grounds), and the fliers do all their work on theirs. I read the card as advice. It's actually describing the holds' danger to Rook. **The writing pass should make it read as a warning** ("their bows can ground her"). That's a text fix, not a board fix. The pursuit's roster is announced on the first card, so part of this is my misread.
- **After the turn-2 trade, Rook had no way back.** She carries no item, the dressing heals only its user, and from turn 5 on every tile in her reach had at least one 54% striker. I scanned all 150 tiles twice. So turns 5 and 6 weren't choices: they were a list sorted by how many enemies could reach each tile. The 14,1 dodge on the Recall was a coin (the wingrider missed and her counter took him to 7). Then 14,6 was the best of a bad list (60 x 54), and it came up. That's tense, but it's the reason choice scores 5.
- I never weighed the fort at 11,3 (heals 4 a phase, inside the sentry's bow ring). A cold chair might find a line there that I didn't.

**What I'd do with it: no lever yet.** This read is dominated by two of my own choices: an ally who can't answer the pursuit, and a turn-2 trade that left Rook at 4 for the rest of the map. Both are legitimate, and both say something about the map. But neither says the ring is wrong, and the ring is what #862 asked about. The next read should be **the Critic, on a fresh seed, with an ally who counters** (Wren, Dunstan or Teodor), so the gate gets read on the map and not on my pick. If that chair also finds that the early captain kill leaves Rook nothing to do but sort tiles, my lean for the lever is a reward for the kill in the loft, keyed to the captain falling: something Rook can reach to recover, so taking the master early buys a turn of choice instead of a long dodge. I'm not filing it on one play.

## 2026-10-05 — The field, Rook's pick, fresh seed, warm — Code
Seed: 1340 (`campaign --seed 1340 --from the_field --pick rook --level 5 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_field-1340.script`; transcript beside it; main at 49ded26). Result: **won on turn 13 of 20.** Nobody fell. One Recall spent (turn 5). Keziah was turned by Rook's talk on turn 2.
Disclosures: warm. This is my third Rook arm (81, 973), and I built `seen_far`. This is the fresh seed DIALOGUE said I owed: Rook's arm goes `tuned` if it reads surprise 7. To keep the read honest I didn't replay 973's route. I opened in the other forest (4,14), and I took the line first and the north crossing, where 973 went south. The Recall followed 0238: the rolls stayed, and I changed the plan, not the dice.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 12. The boss had stepped off his fort onto 18,8. Full Measure from 18,9 was certain for 16, and Rook from 17,8 could add 5 x2. That's 26 against his 27, and no third tile was in anyone's reach. Teodor at 17,8 instead left him at 3. Either second striker took his 67 to 82 percent counter and stayed beside him at single digits. So I swung Full Measure alone (24 percent crit for the kill, no crit), parked the rest one tile outside his walk, and let `threat` show every tile he'd refuse. He went home to the fort at 11. On turn 13 Pell's Cinder read 91 for exactly 11 from 18,8, and it landed.
Notes:
- **Turn 1: the lure works from the other forest too.** Rook at 4,14 woke the pickets out of their reach. Keziah came to 4,12, the archer to 6,12, the soldier and brigand behind. The turn-2 talk from 3,12 cost nothing. Three entries now show this opening, so it's the arm's opening, not a trick of one tile.
- **Turn 4: Rook woke the line from the forest at 11,9**, 6 from soldier-2, with nothing able to reach her. That fixed the route north, and on enemy phase 5 the drift sent the rider to 12,8. **Turn 5 was the mistake.** Rook took the archer at 64 percent for the kill, hit once, and `threat` priced her at 35 against 18 among three of them. I spent the Recall on that, and pulled everyone back out of reach instead.
- **Turn 6 was the most tense phase of the play.** Two soldiers came at a party that had no tile to keep the captain single-sided. I built the wall so the captain could take only one strike. Teodor at 9,9 was open to both soldiers at 52 percent lethal. Both missed, and both took 10 back. That was fair dice, and I knew the price going in.
- **Surprise 1: the drift's rider didn't stop at the crossing.** It came over 12,8 and down to 11,11, put Ottilie on 2 (11 of her 13), and left. It was the one piece of the map I'd stopped watching.
- **Surprise 2: the boss sallied when his camp woke.** Rook at 15,12 woke the camp on turn 9. The archer came to 15,10, the brigand to 16,8, and the Sworn Captain walked off his fort to 18,10. With the party at 5 and more, `threat` showed him refusing every strike tile, and he went home on enemy phase 10. On enemy phase 11 he stepped out to 18,8 again, and that's the board turn 12 was played on. The camp didn't wake on noise this time, for the first time in six chairs, because the lure got there first.
- **Rook's counter crit the brigand** (5 percent, 30) on enemy phase 10. That was luck, and it spared a turn.
- **Not tense:** turns 3 and 4 (cleanup, then a walk), and turns 8 to 11, the march round the south bank to the camp. That's shorter than the old stretch and has the lure in it, but turn 11 was empty.
- **Verdict:** 7+ on all three, with the best turn named. With Chat's 5150 at 7/7/7, this meets DIALOGUE's condition, and Rook's arm is `tuned` (0246).

## 2026-10-05 — The Tollgate, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1320 (`play the_tollgate --seed 1320 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_tollgate-1320.script`; transcript beside it; main at 383f36d)   Result: **won on turn 8 of 10.** Nobody fell and no Recall was spent. The boss was left at 8.
Warm: this is my tenth or so Tollgate, and I built the rider trigger. It's the third tuned map replayed under 0237's no-spike rule, after Harrow Weir (1300) and Brackwater Cut (1310).
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 7. The warden was dead and the archer at 6,1 was the only door to the gate: 7,1's other neighbours are the boss and the wall. Wren went first from 5,1 (85 for 10), because what the captain could spend depended on her roll. If she missed, Full Measure was a certain 19 for the kill, and it would cost the captain turn 8 beside the boss's 62 for 16. If she hit, Feint at 99 for 9 finished the archer and kept his legs. She hit, the Feint landed, and the boss's one swing put the captain at 6 and took 18 back. On turn 8 he walked 6,1 to the gate.
Notes:
- **Turns 1 to 3 were a walk, and the woods pair died in one turn.** Pell and Teodor's 47 percent took the brigand, and Wren and the captain the archer, with every swing landing. That's luck, not the map. 739 spent six misses on the same brigand. The one cost was the brigand's counter to Pell for 13, which left her at 3 for the rest of the map and kept her out of every later turn but one.
- **The rider was a gift this time.** I stepped Teodor onto 6,4 on turn 4 with Pell already out of its Mov. The rider took the one target it had, ate Teodor's 76 percent counter, and died to Pell's 99 on turn 5. The trigger keeps the corridor from being free, but once it's priced it's a kill.
- **Surprise: the Toll Axe over the wall.** On enemy phase 6 the boss struck Teodor at 7,4 from 7,2, at range 2 across the corridor wall, for 11 (12 to 1). `threat teodor from 7,4` would have printed exactly that, and the transcript asks it after the fact. My mistake, not the screen's. It's the same hand axe that 739 met on turn 7, from the other side.
- **My mistake twice, both with a dressing in the pack.** On turn 5 Teodor's Long Thrust missed, the warden's counter put him at 2, and I ended into a printed `Lethal if all land: Teodor`. The warden's 31 percent missed. I should have spent the dressing instead of the wait.
- **The cork still works.** The warden on 6,2 behind the one-tile gap took two turns and both of the corridor's tiles (6,3 for a sword, 6,4 for range 2). Wren's Heavy Cut left him at 2, and he died to her counter on his own phase.
- **The archer and the boss never moved**, and that's the map. The keep holds, so 6,3, 6,2 and 7,4 are a priced kill zone and not a swarm.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. The soft approach (turns 1 to 3) is the same note as every Tollgate entry, and it's the map's shape, not a bug.

## 2026-10-05 — The field on Keziah's pick, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1350 (`campaign --seed 1350 --from the_field --pick keziah --fed 10 --level 5 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_field-1350.script`; transcript beside it; main at c58859b)   Result: **won on turn 13 of 20.** Nobody fell. One Recall spent (turn 12). Rook was turned on turn 2 at 4 HP.
Warm: I built the field and played it on this arm as 936. It's the fourth tuned board replayed under 0237's no-spike rule, after Harrow Weir (1300), Brackwater Cut (1310) and the Tollgate (1320), and the first on this arm.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 1. I opened differently from 936. Instead of creeping up to talk distance, I woke the pickets on purpose, with Teodor on 4,12 as bait, so Rook would fly in to me. First, though, `threat keziah` from 3,13 printed the line that set the whole opening: Rook from 4,13, and Keziah's Kinsbane counter "kills on hit". The claimant's own counter could kill the claimant. So Keziah waited at 1,13, out of the drake's reach, and everyone else moved off the tiles a flier would pick for a free hit. Rook took Teodor, ate his 14 counter and dropped to 4. On turn 2 Teodor stepped off, and Keziah walked into his tile and talked her round before anything on my side could finish her.
Notes:
- **Kinsbane is a clock on this arm, and it pushed.** "Hungry: -5 HP at the next phase start" drained her on turns 2, 5 and 6. Every turn the question was whether she could reach a kill. On turn 6 she took the rider from 15,14 at 76 percent. The fifth tooth grew, the blade woke ("Keziah. I remember what I'm for."), and the hunt's free Move took her back out of the south group's ground. That was the best single moment of the map.
- **Surprise: the camp woke on an enemy phase, and that's on me.** On enemy phase 8 the line group's archer shot Teodor from 11,8. That is six tiles from the camp archer on 17,8, so the noise woke the camp. I had counted noise from my own fights, never from the shots I'd let them take. The rule is printed ("a combat happens within 6"), and the attacker's tile counts. It's geometry, not a hidden rule, so I'm not filing it.
- **The guard boss came off his fort twice.** On enemy phase 8 he went to 18,10 on open plain, and on phase 10 to 15,6 beside the sentry. Both times it was out of anyone's reach, and both times `threat` showed him refusing every strike tile and heading home. The windows were real, but I couldn't reach either one. It's the approach rule doing its job, and it made me count his walk.
- **Turn 12 was the tense one, and it cost the Recall.** Ottilie chipped him to 25, and then Keziah's 58 percent on the fort missed. His counter left her at 8, adjacent and with no move left. The captain's Full Measure was 99 for 13 (23 crit), which wouldn't kill. His counter was 72 percent for 11 against 12 HP, and Full Measure would have left him unable to move away. I recalled to the turn's start (the rolls stay the same, and the plan changes), healed, and used the turn to bring Pell and Teodor into reach. On turn 13 Pell's Overcast hit for 16 at 91, and Full Measure finished him at 99. Paying a Recall plus a turn is exactly what the keep is meant to collect.
- **The drift held.** The south group (the rider) woke first, and the line group marched down column 12 for 12,14. They arrived strung out, a soldier and then the archer, and died across turns 8 and 9. Pell's crit for 42 was luck.
- **Not tense:** turns 4, 5 and 7. With the pickets dead and both other groups asleep, those were walks. Turn 7 was the third free turn in four.
- **The sentry never mattered**, except for 6 a turn of bow chip on the captain while he stood within 2 of 15,7.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. The empty turns 4 to 7 are the same mid-map march 936 noted. The drift fills it only once a group wakes, and this time I woke one late on purpose.

I placed everyone else and re-checked after each move: he still came. He took 10 off Teodor and stood on open plain. Dunstan hit him for 9, Teodor's Long Thrust from his own tile for 7, and the captain's Full Measure for 17 at 100. By the veto's count he was never exposed there. The veto prices plain weapons, so the two arts are invisible to it. That's #1067, for the Table.

- **I let the rider spawn.** 1300 blocked 7,0 with Teodor. This time I waited for it with Teodor on 5,3 (Horsebane) and Ottilie pulled out of its reach, since a bow can't answer at range 1. It took Teodor's counter, and Pell finished it from range 2.

I recalled and swung Keziah again, expecting a fresh roll. The screen had already told me "the same attack will roll the same". The second Recall bought the plan I should have made the first time: Ottilie, then Dunstan from 9,6, then Teodor's Long Thrust from 8,6 for the kill. Two charges for one lesson. - **The shieldbearer and the archer never acted.** That's the same as 1300's note about the archer.
- **Filed:** #1066, `1 charges left` after the second Recall, a CLI plural bug in 73 transcripts. #1067, the veto doesn't price arts, a question rather than a bug. My lean is to keep it: baiting the veto is meant to be a skill.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands. The win came five turns early because the woken boss approached into an art kill. If the Table prices arts (#1067), this line closes and the crest puzzle is back.

Seed: 1370 (`play brackwater_cut --seed 1370 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-brackwater_cut-1370.script`; transcript beside it; main at 912987d)   Result: **won on turn 8 of 8, four out of five.** Rook, Pell, Wren and the captain escaped. Tension: 8/10   Choice: 8/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 4. On turn 3 Wren stopped on 14,4, four from the shieldbearer, on purpose. The bank woke with its lamps lit and, being Guard turned Aggressive, walked off the exits toward her: the shieldbearer to 14,5, the soldier to 16,3, the brawler into the dark at 13,7. On turn 4 the soldier stood in sight on open plain. Pell's Overcast from 15,2 was 99 for 17 with no counter, and the captain's Feint at 99 for 7 finished it, keeping Full Measure. Notes:
- **Turn 1 was a clean opening.** Rook's 54 for 7 on the fort archer landed, and Pell's Cinder from 10,2 (91 for 10) killed it before it shot anyone. 1310 missed the same 54 twice.

Pell's second Overcast (99 for 18) killed the shieldbearer on turn 5 and opened him a road.

That's my mistake. I knew it was the brawler and that Rook has Def 1. The Recall (0238) bought the knowledge: the captain took 14,2 instead, and Rook flew to 19,3 and left on turn 6.
- **Turn 7 cost the second Recall.** Pell on 19,3 took the brawler's 94 for 9 x2 against 16. Replayed with the knowledge, Pell went to 19,6, five steps from the brawler's 16,3, so it couldn't reach a seat beside her. Wren took 19,5 and spent a dressing to 20, so the brawler's 14 couldn't kill her. The brawler went for the captain instead (one 6 landed, and his counter left it at 2). Then the hexer came out of the dark at 98 for 11. The captain exited on 5 of 22. That was the tensest phase of the play, and there was nothing more to spend on it.
- **Dunstan was the price.** At 6 HP after the brigand's 9, he ran with the rest on turn 6 and the rider caught him on 17,3. I couldn't find a line that kept the cork and got him out too. `count:` had already said his last start was turn 5.
- **Not tense:** turn 2, the walk through the gap. That's one turn of soft stretch, where 1310 had two.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. Luring the bank is a real second line beside 1310's sneak north, and it cost a body and two Recalls. Neither line is free, so this is a choice, not a solve. It's the second 0237 play running to file nothing (after 1350), but #1066 to #1068 came from today's other plays, so 0237's two-day tripwire is nowhere near.

## 2026-10-05 — The Tollgate, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1380 (`play the_tollgate --seed 1380 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_tollgate-1380.script`; transcript beside it; main at 1bfceb4)   Result: **won on turn 10 of 10.** Nobody fell, no Recall was spent, and every enemy died, the boss included.
Warm: my second Tollgate under 0237 (after 1320) and I built the rider trigger. This time I came at the woods from the east (Pell and Teodor up the 8 and 9 columns) instead of 1320's west pair.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 8. The warden was dead and the archer at 6,1 was the last door. Wren went in first from 6,2 at 85 for 10, with the captain's Feint (99 for 9) to finish. She missed. That left two lines: the captain's plain swing at 87 for 11, which leaves the archer alive and a seize on 9 or 10 depending on rolls, or Full Measure at 100 for 19, a certain kill that spends turn 9 and puts the seize on the last turn of the map. I took the certain kill (it crit for 57). For the first time in any of my Tollgate plays, the turn limit was the price of a decision and not a formality.
Notes:
- **Turns 1 and 2 were a walk again.** The woods pair holds, so the approach only decides which side you strike from. It's the map's shape, the same note as every Tollgate entry.
- **Pell's opener costs her the map, for the second replay running.** Gust on the brigand at 95 for 12 drew the 54 percent counter for 13, and she spent turns 3 to 10 at 3 HP. On 1320 it was the same brigand and the same 13. She was still the map's best unit: the rider at 100 for 11 with no counter on turn 6 and the boss at 100 for 11 with no counter on turn 9, both from range 2.
- **The rider is a gift once priced, twice now.** Teodor stopped on 6,4 to Long Thrust the warden, the rider spawned, `threat` showed it would take Teodor at 39 percent into a 76 percent counter, and that is what it did. Pell finished it the next turn. 1320 said the same. The trigger keeps the corridor from being free, but a player who reads `threat` turns it into experience.
- **The cork took three turns.** Two Long Thrusts from 6,4 (76 for 7 each, both hit) and the captain's Feint from 6,3 at 99 for exactly the 7 left. Only one unit can stand on each of 6,4 and 6,3, so the corridor sets the pace, not the damage.
- **Turn 7's stand was the tense one.** The captain on 6,3 after the warden's kill read `If all land: 16 against 17 hp` from the archer and the boss's Toll Axe over the wall. Both missed.
- **The boss died to his own counter's absence.** Wren's double counter on enemy phase 8 put him at 10, and Pell's Gust from 6,3 needed no roll. With no enemy left, the map still asked for the walk to 7,1, and the CLI said so.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. The rider as a priced kill is the second entry to say so; if Chat's next Tollgate says it too, it is a content note for the Table, not a bug.

Seed: 1390 (`campaign --seed 1390 --from the_field --pick rook --level 5 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_field-1390.script`; transcript beside it; main at fe97718)   Result: **won on turn 15 of 20.** Nobody fell and no Recall was spent. Keziah was turned by Rook's talk on turn 2.
Warm: my fourth Rook arm (81, 973, 1340), and I built `seen_far` and the drift. It's the fifth tuned board replayed under 0237. Turn 1 is 1340's, on purpose (the arm's opening); from turn 2 on I played the other line: the south crossing, where 1340 went north.
Tension: 7/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 15. The boss sat on his fort at 24 of 27. Pell's Cinder from 19,7 took him to 13, then Maud missed her 87 and Ottilie missed both of her 68s. Teodor's 58 from 18,7 hit for 6 and ate the 82 percent counter (8 left), leaving 7. The fort changed the captain's choice. On open ground Feint is the 100 percent finisher, but here it read 98 for 4 x2 and needed both hits (96). Full Measure read 99 for 14. I took Full Measure, it landed, and the map ended before the phase it costs.
Notes:
- **The pickets fell by turn 3 again.** Rook's 4,14 lure, the turn-2 talk, and the captain's crit on the archer (12 then 36). Teodor took both of the brigand's and the soldier's strikes at 4,13 and dropped to 5. That's the fourth entry with this opening, and it's the arm's opening, not a tile trick.
- **Turn 5 was the price of the south route.** Rook woke the rider from 10,14 at 52 for 13 and missed both of her 80 percent counters, so she was at 5. Pell's Overcast and the captain's Feint killed it on turn 6. The drift then sent the line group down column 12 for 12,14, but they arrived one at a time: soldier-2 first onto Teodor's bait at 10,13, then the archer, then soldier-1. They died across turns 8 and 9 without reaching two of mine at once. **The south route turns the drift into a conveyor.** 1340's north route met the drifted rider as a surprise. Here the drifted group was a queue. It's one play, so I'm logging it as a note, not filing it as a lever.
- **The boss sallied when Rook woke the camp, the third chair in a row to see it.** On enemy phase 11 he walked to 18,10. `threat` showed him refusing every strike tile, and he went home on phase 12. This time he never came out again, so turns 13 and 14 were a siege. The fort's heal (5) and Def made Ottilie's bow 2 x2 and Rook's lance 3 x2 into a lethal counter, which leaves the kill to magic and the captain's Full Measure. The choice of who goes in is real.
- **Not tense:** turns 4, 9 and 10 (walks), and turns 13 and 14 (setting up the siege, with nothing able to strike anyone).
- **Surprise is a 6 because nothing here was new to me.** The drift, the sally and the fort all played as on 1340, just from another bank.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. The south route's queue is a note for the cold chair Rook's arm owes. If that chair also takes the south and finds the drift a queue, the content lever is the drifted group's spacing (or its wake turn), not the route.

## 2026-10-05 — The field on Keziah's pick, the north route, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1400 (`campaign --seed 1400 --from the_field --pick keziah --fed 10 --level 5 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_field-1400.script`; transcript beside it; main at 9d51e6d)   Result: **won on turn 13 of 20.** Nobody fell for good. Two Recalls spent, both on turn 4. Rook was turned on turn 3 at 4 HP.
Warm: my third play of this arm, after 936 and 1350. 1350 went south; this one went north on purpose.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 13. The boss had walked off his fort to 15,6, on open plain beside his brigand. Keziah walked to 15,8, then Maud stepped to 14,8 and healed her to 17 first, so the brigand's 13 counter could no longer kill her on a miss. She hit for 22, the fifth tooth grew, the blade woke ("Keziah. I remember what I'm for."), and the hunt's free Move took her off 15,8. That freed the tile Ottilie needed. Pell's Overcast with Gust (100 for 17), Ottilie's 4 x2, and the captain's Full Measure finished him. Feint would have done at 2 HP. The map ended, so it cost nothing, but it was the wrong button.
Notes:
- **Turn 2: I tried to talk her round early and the screen refused it for me.** Keziah could reach 7,12 and talk, but `threat` priced the pickets at 31 against her 20. With Maud's Salve it was 31 against 25, and then Maud herself stood at 20 against 18. So I baited instead, with the captain on 4,12 this time, not Teodor. Rook still took Teodor and dropped to 4, exactly as on 1350. Two different baits ended on the same board, so the opening steers you.
- **Turn 3: Commander's Word decided the talk.** Rook stood at 4 HP, and any counter of mine would kill her for good. Keziah was five tiles away with Mov 4. The captain stepped to 5,13 and called press, which reached her and Teodor. She walked the fifth tile and talked Rook round before the enemy phase could get her killed. It's an order that bound, and a move I can name for it, on a campaign map. That's evidence for 13.2's keep clause.
- **Turn 4 cost two Recalls, and the second was my error.** Keziah's 80 percent swing into the brigand (the screen asked for `!`) missed, and his counter killed her. I recalled, had Pell kill the soldier first, and swung Keziah again, believing the order would change the roll. It didn't. The roll is the attack's, as the screen said and as 1360 already taught me. The third plan had the captain's Feint (100) take the brigand and Maud heal Keziah. Nothing filed: the rule is printed and right.
- **The hunger decided the north route.** With no enemy in her reach, the drain took her to 3 HP by turn 11. Maud spent six of her eight Salves on Keziah. On turn 10 the heal came after Keziah's move and before her swing at the rider, and that's the only reason her miss there didn't kill her. That's 13.23's keep clause again: the hunger decided a turn.
- **The drift waited for the route, and the board said so.** I didn't wake the line group until turn 7, so the drift line counted on from turn 5 to 7. Then the rider rode north round the camp's side (16,8, then 12,8) and struck Teodor on turn 9. On this route the drift is one rider arriving late, not a pincer.
- **The boss came off his fort again.** The sentry fell to Pell's Gust (14 x2 at 100), and that woke the camp by noise. The boss then walked to 15,6, where only arts could kill him, and they did. This is the second turn-13 Overcast plus Full Measure finish on this arm (1350 was the other). It's 0251's skill working, not a lever.
- **Not tense:** turns 5, 6 and 11 were walks, the same mid-map march 936 and 1350 noted.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. If the cold chair on this arm goes north, it will meet the same starving walk, and the hunger alone is enough to fill it.

## 2026-10-05 — Harrow Weir, the whole party on the crest, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1410 (`play harrow_weir --seed 1410 --scheme two --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-harrow_weir-1410.script`; transcript beside it; main at 60e42ce)   Result: **won on turn 6 of 15.** Nobody fell, no Recall was spent, and the Foreman never moved or swung.
Warm: my third Harrow replay under 0237, after 1300 and 1360. 1360 won by baiting the Foreman off his hill, and 0251's tripwire watches for a second bait win in a row. I took the north crossing with all six to see what a non-bait line does.
Tension: 5/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 6/10
Best turn: turn 4, and it was a placement turn rather than a strike. With the captain on 12,2 the weir woke, and `threat dunstan` said the Foreman would cross his own bridge (through the shieldbearer on 11,6) to 9,6 and throw at Dunstan on 8,5 from range 2. Moving Ottilie to 9,2 and Dunstan to 9,3 turned every line into "refuses it: too exposed there; holds 13,6". I chose between a bait on the west bank and a siege on the hill, and the screen showed me both before I committed.
Notes:
- **Turns 1 to 3 were the opening every Harrow has.** Pell, Ottilie and the captain killed the wood brigand on turn 1 (87, 56, 63, all landed), and Teodor walked to 7,0 by turn 3. The plug held both the rider and the turn.
- **The archer died first, for free.** On turn 5 Pell's Overcast from 14,2 read 99 for exactly 17. The screen asked for `!` because the counter was lethal 1 time in 100. With the archer gone the Foreman was alone on the east bank.
- **The hill fell in one phase.** On turn 6: Pell's Cinder from 14,5 at 94 for 12 (Overcast would have let him double her, 26 against 16), Ottilie's Aimed Shot from 13,4 at 92 for 5 (his counter left her on 5), and Full Measure from 13,5 at 99 for 16. He went 28, 16, 11, 0. Teodor's Long Thrust and Keziah's axe were the backup, and neither was needed.
- **The brigands from turn 5 never arrived.** The south one stood on 9,7 and the west one on 0,7 when the map ended. Pell's Cinder woke the ford by noise on turn 6, one strike before the end.
- **Not tense:** turns 1 to 3 and 5. The only HP lost all map was Ottilie's 12.
- **Filed:** #1087. Massed on the crest, the party's plain-weapon total is lethal on every tile the Foreman could strike from, so the veto keeps him home, and home is a hill three strikes clear. By DIALOGUE's rule (39, 72) that's scenery. It's for the Table, and my lean is no lever until a cold chair tries it.
- **For 0251's tripwire:** not a bait win, so it does not advance. The Foreman is beatable off his hill (1360) and on it (1410). Both warm lines came in early, on turns 9 and 6.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands on one warm play, but it's the weakest Harrow I've journaled for tension, and #1087 says why.

## 2026-10-05 — Brackwater Cut, the door held from the east, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1420 (`play brackwater_cut --seed 1420 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-brackwater_cut-1420.script`; transcript beside it; main at 43024c4)   Result: **won on turn 8 of 8, two out of five.** Rook and the captain escaped. Dunstan, Pell and Wren fell. One of the three Recalls was spent (turn 4).
Warm: my eleventh Brackwater and the third under 0237. 1310 sneaked north, 1370 lured the bank off the exits. This time I tried a third line: cork the gap from the east side, on 12,3, where only one chaser at a time can stand next to the cork.
Tension: 8/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 7/10
Best turn: turn 6. The rider had come through the gap after Dunstan fell and stood on 14,4 at 11 HP, between the captain and the exits. Pell was on 6 HP with her Cinder spent, one turn from an exit. Running would most likely have got her killed on the edge anyway. Instead she stood still and threw Gust at 100 for exactly 11, the only strike that saved her escape turn, and it crit. That one cast made the captain's walk to 19,8 and Rook's to 19,6 survivable.
Notes:
- **Turn 1 was 1370's opening, and it landed again.** Rook's 54 and Pell's 91 took the fort archer.
- **The east-side cork works against the chase and fails against the bank.** From 12,3 only the gap tile can reach the cork, and the chase queued: one brigand and the archer each phase. But I woke the bank on turn 3 with Wren on the 15,4 forest, and the bank came round behind. On the first turn 4, `end` named Pell lethal (Brawler for 18), and I didn't read it. The brawler killed Pell from 14,3, and the soldier walked into her tile and killed the captain from behind at 12,3. The cork had its back to the bank.
- **The Recall bought the shape, and the rolls stayed honest.** Back at turn 4 with the captain on 14,3, the brawler had no seat next to Pell. It went for Wren in the forest instead, and Dunstan held the door. The brigand missed at 73, and Dunstan's counter crit for 33. Nothing I did changed that roll (keyed rolls). The difference was who stood where.
- **Turn 5 was the tensest phase.** The captain's Feint at 92 missed the brawler on the forest, and its counter took him to 10. Wren swung with `!` at 56 against a 44-in-100 lethal counter and killed it. Then Dunstan, on 2 HP, stayed on the cork on purpose and died to the archer. The rider came through, caught Wren on a 28, and the soldier left Pell on 5.
- **Pell's Cinder ran out mid-map** (`Pell's Cinder is spent for this battle`). Two Overcasts and a counter used seven charges. Gust was what was left, and Gust was enough.
- **Not tense:** turn 2, the walk to the door. That's one soft turn, as in 1370.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. Holding the door is a real third line, and it costs more than the other two: three bodies and a Recall, against 1370's one body and two Recalls. Dusk did its job, too. On turn 7 the soldier couldn't see the captain at 19,8, five tiles off and out of hearing, so it went for Rook instead, and missed. Brackwater has now given me three different lines, each with a different price, and that's what a tuned map should do.

## 2026-10-05 — The Tollgate, a tuned map replayed under 0237, warm — Code
Seed: 1430 (`play the_tollgate --seed 1430 --strict --script docs/transcripts/2026-10-05-the_tollgate-1430.script`; transcript beside it; main at 4ece4cc)   Result: **won on turn 8 of 10.** Nobody fell, nobody lost a hit point, and no Recall was spent. The boss was left standing.
Warm: my third Tollgate under 0237 (after 1320 and 1380), and I built the rider trigger. This time I staged all four just outside both woods turrets' reach on turn 3 (`threat` read clean on every tile) and struck on turn 4, instead of 1380's turn-3 opener.
Tension: 5/10   Choice: 7/10   Surprise: 5/10
Best turn: turn 6. The corridor only fits two attackers on the warden, 6,3 and 6,4. Feint from 6,3 read 99 for 7 and Gust from 6,4 100 for 11, 18 against his 21, so the cork needed Full Measure (100 for 17), and Full Measure freezes the captain on 6,3 for turn 7 with the rider just spawned. The answer was to fill both sides of him: Pell stopped on 6,4 and Teodor walked through to 6,2 and put the archer at 6,1 down to 5. With 6,4 and 6,2 taken, the rider had no seat next to the frozen captain, `threat` on Pell showed it would take her at 39 into Gust's 100 percent counter, and that's what it did. Full Measure crit for 51 anyway, but the shape held whichever way it rolled.
Notes:
- **Why tension is a 5: the dice.** The enemy swung six times all map (the archers at 34 and 67, the boss at 58, 39 and 39, the rider at 39) and missed all six. Roughly 2.8 hits were expected, and all six missing is about a 3 percent run. Add the captain's two crits (Feint for 24 on the brigand, Full Measure for 51 on the warden) and the map never pushed back. That's the seed, not the line. 1380 on a similar shape cost Pell 13 HP and the last turn.
- **The woods pair are turrets, so staging is free.** Hold units never move, and their reach is countable from the board, so four units can stand three tiles off and pick the turn they strike. Every Tollgate entry says turns 1 and 2 are a walk. On this play turn 3 was one too.
- **My mistake, unpunished:** on turn 5 Teodor waited on 7,4, inside the boss's Toll Axe reach over the wall (range 2). I didn't run `threat` on him, and the boss swung at 58 for 11 and missed. The screen would have told me.
- **The rider is a priced kill for the third entry running** (1320, 1380, 1430). 1380 said that if a third entry said so too, it becomes a content note for the Table, not a bug. This is that third entry, but all three are warm and all mine, so the note waits for Chat's next Tollgate, as 1380 put it.
- **The boss was never fought.** Once the archer at 6,1 fell, the gate was three steps from 6,3 through Teodor's tile. A Seize map lets you leave him, and that's right.
- **Verdict:** the tuned verdict stands, and the play files nothing. The corridor's two-tile cork, and filling both sides of whoever corks it, is the map's real decision. The rest was a lucky seed.
